dates earlier that reaches the copies later, a receipt entered the next day
and dated back say, counts, as it does in NetSuite, which costs the
fulfillment again. An item FleetSuite counted none of there can't be
costed.

NetSuite's cost is what its fulfillment took off the inventory accounts, as
the copy of its general ledger has it, line by line.

### The check

`checkOwnFulfillment` (`src/lib/books/own-fulfillment-check.ts`) puts each
copy next to NetSuite's fulfillment as the copy of NetSuite's stock moves
has it (migration 401): its date, where it took stock from, each item and
how many, and what it cost. A copy:

- **waits** until the copy of NetSuite's stock moves has NetSuite's
  fulfillment and the copy of its general ledger has its entry. The stock
  copy takes only documents that move stock, so once it has run since the
  copy was made (its `last_synced_at` in `sync_state`) without the
  fulfillment, NetSuite's took nothing from stock; once the ledger copy has
  finished a whole run more than ten minutes after, with no entry for it,
  NetSuite's booked no cost;
- is **voided** ("Deleted in NetSuite") when NetSuite deleted its
  fulfillment;
- **differs** when anything compared differs;
- is **incomplete** when nothing compared differs but FleetSuite didn't know
  something, which then isn't compared: a kit's parts (what NetSuite took
  that FleetSuite's copy doesn't have may be them), whether an item the
  lists copy doesn't have is kept in stock, where it took stock from, or
  the cost of an item it counted none of;
- **matches** otherwise.

What was taken, each item and how many, is compared apart from where it was
taken from and what it cost. So a fulfillment taken from another location
says so once, and the list's sentence doesn't name the cost too, which
follows from what was taken and from where.

### The pages

`/admin/books/own-fulfillments` (`deepLinks.booksOwnFulfillments`,
`?month=YYYY-MM`, `&only=differs`) lists a month's copies, those that
differ first, with FleetSuite's cost, NetSuite's and the difference, how
many have each status, how the last copies of NetSuite's stock moves,
ledger and lists and the daily check went, and a search. Each opens
`/admin/books/own-fulfillments/fulfillment?id=`
(`deepLinks.booksOwnFulfillment`, by NetSuite's id for the fulfillment):
what's compared, side by side with why it differs; each item with its
quantity, cost each and cost on both sides and the count and value
FleetSuite costed it from, linking to its moves on Stock by item; the
copy's entry next to NetSuite's, or what NetSuite's fulfillment posted,
account by account, when the copy's isn't worked out; what FleetSuite
didn't know; how it worked the copy out; and FleetSuite's own invoice off
the order. Both say what the books hold for it ("Posting FleetSuite's own
fulfillments, receipts and bills"). The daily check tells the super admins
when one stops matching ("Daily check").

### What isn't done yet

- There's no history: copies start the day part 25 shipped.
- FleetSuite doesn't know a kit's parts, so a fulfillment of a kit is
  incomplete.
- A fulfillment NetSuite took from more than one location differs on
  location: FleetSuite takes each one from one.

## FleetSuite's own bills and receipts

From the day part 27 shipped, Books keeps its own copy of each bill
FleetSuite puts into NetSuite, and of each receipt of a purchase order's
items, worked out from what FleetSuite knows rather than read back from
NetSuite (owner decision 2026-10-24, "Shared bills": each bill is entered
once, from FleetSuite, and Books keeps its copy to check against
NetSuite's). The copies are `gl_own_bills` and `gl_own_bill_lines`, and
`gl_own_receipts` and `gl_own_receipt_lines` (migration 435). From part 30
the books post a copy in place of NetSuite's bill or item receipt when it
matches it and makes the same entry ("Posting FleetSuite's own
fulfillments, receipts and bills"), and NetSuite's otherwise ("Vendor
bills", "Stock and cost"). Nothing more is written to NetSuite than
before.

FleetSuite makes bills four ways, and each saves a copy right after
NetSuite makes the bill (`saveOwnBill` in `src/lib/books/own-bills.ts`):

- **Create Bill in NetSuite on an approved CNI vendor invoice**
  (`/api/vendor-invoices/workflow`) and **on an approved installer payout**
  (`/api/admin/payouts`): one expense line, to the account and for the
  amount FleetSuite sent.
- **Create bill on a Parts Mail invoice** (`/api/parts-mail/create-bill`):
  NetSuite makes the bill from the invoice's purchase order, at the PO's
  prices, so the copy's lines are what FleetSuite's copy of the PO
  (`netsuite_vendor_po_lines`) still had to bill, each line's quantity less
  what's billed, at the line's price, to the cent. The vendor's invoice
  total is kept beside them, as the vendor's invoice says it.
- **Send to NetSuite on Snap a bill** (`/api/admin/books/snap-a-bill`):
  each line to the account and for the amount FleetSuite sent, with the
  bill's total beside them ("Snap a bill"). A snapped bill that billed a
  PO is the PO's open lines, as a Parts Mail bill's are, then the line for
  what the bill had beyond them ("PO bills").

A bill is dated the day FleetSuite made it, in Central time: FleetSuite
sends NetSuite no date for a bill, so NetSuite dates it that day. A snapped
bill is the exception: it's dated as the bill is, and FleetSuite sends
NetSuite that date. It's kept for the company whose NetSuite subsidiary it
was made in, else BMG.

Receive on the Receiving page (`/api/po-receipts`) saves a copy of each
Receive (`saveOwnReceipt` in `src/lib/books/own-receipts.ts`), keyed by its
first row in `po_receipts`, as one tap of Receive is one receipt: each item
and how many, at the price FleetSuite's copy of the PO has for its line,
with NetSuite's id for the item receipt when NetSuite took it, or what
NetSuite said when it didn't. It's dated as FleetSuite dated it for
NetSuite, and kept for BMG, as FleetSuite's copy of a PO doesn't say the
company.

Saving never stops a bill or a Receive: a copy that can't be saved is
logged (`[books] own copy of …`) and the work goes on. A copy is kept once;
saving the same one again changes nothing. Bills and receipts made in
NetSuite by hand have no copy. What FleetSuite can't work out is kept on the
copy as unknown, never guessed from NetSuite: a Parts Mail bill's lines (its
copy of the PO didn't have them yet) and a line's price.

### One bill each

Create Bill in NetSuite on a vendor invoice or a payout claims the row first
(`bill_claimed_at`, `makeBillOnce` in `src/lib/bill-once.ts`), as Parts
Mail's bills already did (migration 251), so only one try at a time gets
past it: a double tap, or two people at once, can't make two bills. Then it
asks NetSuite for a bill from that vendor with the same Reference No.
(`findVendorBill` in `src/lib/netsuite.ts`), so a try whose bill NetSuite
made but FleetSuite never heard back about finds that bill rather than
making another; when NetSuite can't be asked, it makes none. A bill NetSuite
refuses lets the claim go, the stamp that the row is billed clears it, and a
claim over 15 minutes old is taken over. Send to NetSuite on Snap a bill
claims the snapped bill the same way, while it's a draft.

### The checks

`checkOwnBill` (`src/lib/books/own-bill-check.ts`) puts each bill copy next
to NetSuite's bill as the copy of NetSuite's bills has it (migration 384):
its vendor, number and date, the company the copy of its general ledger
books it to, each line (an expense line by its account, an item line by its
item, with how many) and its total. NetSuite's tax and layout lines are
left out. `checkOwnReceipt` (`src/lib/books/own-receipt-check.ts`) puts each
receipt copy next to NetSuite's item receipt as the copy of NetSuite's stock
moves has it (migration 401): its date, its company, each item and how
many, and what it added to stock, the items NetSuite keeps in stock at the
PO's prices, to the cent, against what NetSuite's receipt posted to the
inventory accounts, as the copy of its general ledger has it. Where NetSuite
put it is shown, not compared. A copy:

- **waits** until the copies have NetSuite's bill or receipt, and the
  ledger entry that says a bill's company or values a receipt. Once the
  bills copy has run since without the bill, or the stock copy without a
  receipt of anything kept in stock, NetSuite's is gone;
- is **not in NetSuite** (a receipt) when NetSuite didn't take the Receive
  and it's on Receiving's list to enter by hand there;
- is **voided** ("Gone from NetSuite") when NetSuite's is gone, deleted or
  voided;
- **differs** when anything compared differs;
- is **incomplete** when nothing compared differs but FleetSuite didn't know
  something, which then isn't compared, or when there's nothing to check
  against: a receipt entered in NetSuite by hand (Receiving doesn't say
  which item receipt that is), or one NetSuite didn't say it made;
- **matches** otherwise.

A receipt's items and how many are compared apart from what it added to
stock, so another count says so once, and the list's sentence doesn't name
the cost too, which follows from it; a bill's total likewise follows from
its lines.

### The pages

`/admin/books/own-bills` (`deepLinks.booksOwnBills`, `?month=YYYY-MM`,
`&only=differs`) lists a month's bill copies, those that differ first, with
FleetSuite's total, NetSuite's and the difference and what each was made
from, how many have each status, how the last copies of NetSuite's bills,
ledger and lists and the daily check went, and a search. Each opens
`/admin/books/own-bills/bill?id=` (`deepLinks.booksOwnBill`, by NetSuite's
id for the bill): what's compared, side by side with why it differs, each
line on both sides, what FleetSuite didn't know and how it worked the copy
out, linking to the vendor invoice, payout or Parts Mail it was made from,
or the picture it was snapped from and who snapped it, and to the bill and
PO in NetSuite. `/admin/books/own-receipts` (`deepLinks.booksOwnReceipts`)
lists a month's receipt copies the same way,
with what each added to stock on both sides. Each opens
`/admin/books/own-receipts/receipt?key=` (`deepLinks.booksOwnReceipt`, by
the copy's key): what's compared, each item with its count and value on
both sides, linking to its moves on Stock by item, where Receiving's rows
for it stand, and what NetSuite said when it didn't take it. Each list and
page says what the books hold for it, and each page puts the copy's entry
next to NetSuite's ("Posting FleetSuite's own fulfillments, receipts and
bills"). The daily check tells the super admins when one stops matching
("Daily check").

### What isn't done yet

- There's no history: copies start the day part 27 shipped.
- FleetSuite's copy of a PO doesn't say the company, so a Parts Mail bill
  and a receipt are kept for BMG, and one for 1084 differs on its company.
- Receiving dates a receipt by the day in UTC, which in the evening, Central
  time, is already the next day, and NetSuite takes that date.
- Parts Mail's own claim on its bills doesn't ask NetSuite for the bill
  before making one, and isn't taken over when it's left behind.

## FleetSuite's own adjustments

From part 31 (owner decision 2026-10-30, "Own postings", then "Build it",
its second piece) Books checks each change made with Inventory Adjust
against NetSuite's inventory adjustment. Inventory Adjust (migration 454,
`src/lib/inventory-adjust.ts`) makes the adjustment in NetSuite and keeps a
row of it in `inventory_adjustments`: the item, where, how many more or
fewer, how it was entered (a number added or taken off, or what was on the
shelf), the reason, the note, who made it and NetSuite's id and number for
the adjustment. That row is Books' own copy, so no table is added: migration
457 only reads it (`gl_own_adjustment_pairs` and `gl_own_adjustment_months`).
Each is BMG's, as Inventory Adjust adjusts O'Fallon's stock, and is dated
the day it was made, in Central time.

FleetSuite values the change itself, as it costs its own fulfillments ("What
it cost"): at the item's average at that location just before, the value
FleetSuite counted of it there over the count, from its count and value on
December 31, 2024 and every NetSuite document since that comes before the
adjustment, by date and then NetSuite's id, to the cent. An item NetSuite
doesn't keep in stock comes to nothing. The other side goes to 52000
Inventory Adjustment, the account Inventory Adjust posts to.

### The check

`checkOwnAdjustment` (`src/lib/books/own-adjustment-check.ts`) puts each
one next to NetSuite's adjustment as the copy of NetSuite's stock moves has
it (migration 401), valued from the copy of its general ledger: its date,
where it changed stock, the item and by how many, what that came to against
what NetSuite's adjustment posted to the inventory accounts, and the account
on its other side. An Inventory Adjust:

- is **incomplete** when NetSuite didn't say which adjustment it made, so
  there's none to put it next to;
- **waits** until the stock copy (every two hours) has NetSuite's adjustment
  and the ledger copy has its entry. Once the stock copy has run since
  without it, NetSuite's is gone ("Gone from NetSuite"), as it is when
  NetSuite deleted it: an adjustment always moves stock. Once the ledger copy
  has run since with no entry for it, NetSuite's booked nothing;
- **differs** when anything compared differs;
- is **incomplete** when nothing compared differs but FleetSuite couldn't
  work something out (where it changed stock, whether the item is kept in
  stock, or a value when it counted none there just before), which then
  isn't compared;
- **matches** otherwise.

The value follows from the change and where it was made, so when either
differs the list's sentence doesn't name the value too.

### The pages

`/admin/books/own-adjustments` (`deepLinks.booksOwnAdjustments`,
`?month=YYYY-MM`, `&only=differs`) lists a month's Inventory Adjusts, those
that differ first, with what each change came to on FleetSuite's side, on
NetSuite's and the difference (stock taken out in brackets), the reason and
who made it, what the books hold for it, how many have each status, how the
last copies of NetSuite's stock moves, ledger and lists and the daily check
went, and a search. Each opens `/admin/books/own-adjustments/adjustment?id=`
(`deepLinks.booksOwnAdjustment`, by FleetSuite's id for it): what's
compared, side by side with why it differs, the copy's entry next to
NetSuite's, account by account ("Posting FleetSuite's own fulfillments,
receipts and bills"), what FleetSuite didn't know and how it worked the
value out, linking to the item's moves on Stock by item and to the
adjustment in NetSuite. Invoices vs NetSuite links each adjustment it lists
that has an Inventory Adjust to it. The daily check tells the super admins
when one stops matching ("Daily check").

### Posting

The books post an Inventory Adjust in place of NetSuite's adjustment when it
matches it and makes the same entry, with `own_adjustment@1`, keyed as
NetSuite's is (`inventory_adjustment:<NetSuite id>:<n>`), as they post the
own fulfillments and receipts ("Posting FleetSuite's own fulfillments,
receipts and bills"). One NetSuite didn't say it made posts nothing from
FleetSuite: NetSuite's adjustment posts as NetSuite booked it.

### What isn't done yet

- There's no history: Inventory Adjust began on 2026-10-30, and an
  adjustment made in NetSuite by hand has no Inventory Adjust, so it posts
  from NetSuite's.
- NetSuite values an adjustment at its own average cost, which a document
  dated earlier and entered later can move after the fact; FleetSuite's
  average comes from the copies as they stand, so the two can differ by
  cents, and NetSuite's posts.

## Posting FleetSuite's own fulfillments, receipts and bills

From part 30 (owner decision 2026-10-30, "Own postings", then "Build it")
the books post Books' own copy of a fulfillment FleetSuite made, of a
Receive NetSuite took and of a bill FleetSuite put into NetSuite, snapped
bills among them, in place of NetSuite's document, as they post its own
invoices ("Posting FleetSuite's own invoices"), and from part 31 a change
made with Inventory Adjust too ("FleetSuite's own adjustments"), when:

- the copy **matches** NetSuite's document, as Own fulfillments, Own
  receipts, Own adjustments or Own bills checks it; and
- the entry the copy makes is the one NetSuite's makes: the same amount to
  each account, on the same date, and for a bill the payables for the same
  vendor.

Otherwise NetSuite's document posts, as it always has: when the copy
differs, is incomplete or is waiting, and when its entry can't be worked
out or comes out different. The second test means switching copies never
moves anything in the books, so Differences, the reports, the ledger on
Stock by item, the balances and the daily check read the same whichever
copy posted. A copy that posts and then stops matching (NetSuite's
document was changed, say) goes back to NetSuite's at the next run, and
the other way round. A document whose own lines in NetSuite can't be worked
out, or are caught mid-refresh, keeps whatever entry stands.

The copies' entries (`src/lib/books/own-stock-posting.ts` and
`src/lib/books/own-bill-posting.ts`):

- **A fulfillment** (`own_fulfillment@1`): each item's cost, as Own
  fulfillments works it out (its average at the location just before),
  comes off the item's asset account, as the copy of NetSuite's items has
  it, and goes to its cost of goods account. NetSuite won't give an item's
  cost of goods account, so it's the account NetSuite's last fulfillment of
  the item before this one debited its cost to, or, for an item NetSuite
  hasn't fulfilled before, the one most of the company's fulfillments debit
  theirs to (`gl_own_stock_accounts`, migration 456). When NetSuite's
  fulfillment put an item's cost in another account, its page says so, and
  the item's next fulfillment takes NetSuite's account from it.
- **A receipt** (`own_receipt@1`): each item NetSuite keeps in stock goes
  onto its asset account at the purchase order's price, as Own receipts
  works it out, and what they come to is credited to the account the
  company's item receipts hold received stock against until it's billed,
  the one most of them credit, as the bill rule picks it.
- **A bill** (`own_bill@1`): the bill rule's working on the copy's own
  lines, with the accounts NetSuite's bill is worked out with: payables take
  the copy's total and carry its vendor, an expense line goes to its
  account, and an item line to where NetSuite puts the item, a stock item
  from a purchase order's line to the account received stock is held
  against until it's billed.
- **An adjustment** (`own_adjustment@1`): what the change came to, as Own
  adjustments works it out (the item's average at the location just
  before), goes onto the item's asset account, or comes off it, against
  52000 Inventory Adjustment, the account Inventory Adjust posts to.

Each is dated as NetSuite's is, in the month NetSuite booked the document
into, keyed as NetSuite's is (`item_fulfillment:<NetSuite id>:<n>`,
`item_receipt:…`, `inventory_adjustment:…`, `vendor_bill:…`), and its lines
name the copy's own (`own:<item>:cost`, `own:<item>:asset`, `own:received`,
`own:adjustment`, `own:<line>`). Fulfillments and adjustments are BMG's; a
receipt or a bill posts for the company it's kept for, and one NetSuite
booked to both companies posts from NetSuite's.

The posting run reads the copies of each month's fulfillments, receipts,
adjustments and bills, made on the days they're dated, from the first month
Books made any, and checks each as the Own pages do, against the copies of
NetSuite's stock moves, bills and general ledger as far as they had come.
Migration 456 has `gl_stock_postings` and `gl_bill_postings` say which rule
made the entry that stands (`posting_rule`), so a document whose entry was
made by the other copy's rule has it replaced, reversed and posted again in
one transaction, the reversal's memo saying why ("IF-1234 now posts from
FleetSuite's own copy"). So the first run after part 30 shipped replaced the
entry of each document whose copy matched, and the first after part 31 that
of each adjustment, and each lists NetSuite's as an earlier entry. A read of
the copies that fails stops the run, saying what it was reading.

Own fulfillments, Own receipts, Own adjustments and Own bills show on each
row what the books hold for NetSuite's document ("Entry 902 · own copy",
"Entry 871 · NetSuite's", "Not posted yet") and count them above the list.
Each one's page says which copy made its entry, what the next run does about
it and, when NetSuite's posts, why, with a link to the document on Invoices
vs NetSuite, and puts the copy's entry next to NetSuite's, account by
account, with what differs in red. On Invoices vs NetSuite each one posted
from its copy reads "Entry 902 · own copy", its month counts how many did,
and opening it links to its copy.

### What isn't done yet

- Transfers, builds and unbuilds and invoices' cost lines post from
  NetSuite's: Books keeps no copies of them yet. So do inventory
  adjustments made in NetSuite by hand, which have no Inventory Adjust.
- Bill credits and bill payments post from NetSuite's. A Receive NetSuite
  didn't take posts nothing until it's entered there by hand, and then
  NetSuite's receipt posts, as Receiving doesn't know which it is.
- An item's cost of goods account is learned from NetSuite's fulfillments,
  so after NetSuite moves an item to another account, its next fulfillment
  posts from NetSuite's, and the one after takes the new account.

## Snap a bill

From the day part 28 shipped, a super admin, and since part 52 the
bookkeeper, can enter a vendor's bill in NetSuite from a picture of it
(owner decision 2026-10-25, "Snap a bill", as Books Previews drew it). Books
→ Snap a bill (`/admin/books/snap-a-bill`, `deepLinks.booksSnapBill`) takes
a picture with the phone's camera, or a PDF or picture picked from the phone
or computer. FleetSuite reads it, they check each box against the bill, and
Send to NetSuite enters it as a vendor bill for BMG or 1084, once only.
Books keeps its own copy, as it does for the other bills FleetSuite makes
("FleetSuite's own bills and receipts"). Each snapped bill is a row of
`gl_snapped_bills` (migration 436), from its picture to NetSuite's bill.

### The picture

The page turns an iPhone's HEIC picture into a JPEG and shrinks a photo to
2400 pixels on its longest side before it uploads it
(`shrinkPhotoForUpload`), then uploads it as record files go
(`uploadRecordFile`): straight to storage, else through the route. A picture
is taken up to 5MB and a PDF up to 20MB, the most the reader takes. It's
kept under the storage prefix `books-bills`, named by a random id, which
`/api/storage` never serves (`src/lib/storage-guard.ts`): only the route
does (`?file=`, `deepLinks.booksSnapBillFile`), to the bookkeeper and super
admins, never cached. A bill thrown away is kept, marked thrown away, with
its picture.

### Reading it

`readBill` (`src/lib/books/bill-reader.ts`) sends the picture or PDF to
Claude (`claude-opus-5-5`, or the model `BILL_READER_MODEL` names, with
`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) and asks for a fixed shape back: whether it's a
vendor's bill at all, the vendor's name, the bill number, its date and due
date, its terms, the PO number it names, each charge with its amount, its
total, and what it says is due. What the reader couldn't read is left empty, never guessed: a total it
couldn't read too, rather than the lines added up, since the lines are
checked against it. It keeps a bill's first 200 lines, the most the form
takes, so a longer statement comes up short of its total. When the model
declines a bill, the API reads it again with the model Anthropic recommends
(server-side fallbacks); a busy API is tried again (`callAnthropicWithRetry`).
A read costs about 5 to 10 cents. A read that fails says why on the page,
with Read it again; one that isn't a bill says so, and the form can still be
filled in by hand. A read fills in the form only if nobody saved or sent it
while it read.

### The suggestions

From what was read, `formFromRead` (`src/lib/books/snap-a-bill.ts`) fills in
the form:

- **The vendor**: the vendor in the copy of NetSuite's vendor list whose
  name has the same words (`matchVendor`). A close match is shown in amber
  with the others like it to pick from. The vendor box searches the copy,
  and NetSuite itself for a vendor made since the copy last ran.
- **The company**: the one the copy of the general ledger books most of the
  vendor's bills to, else BMG. A note says when their bills went to the
  other one.
- **The location**: where the vendor's bills in the company usually go,
  else `NETSUITE_DEFAULT_LOCATION_ID`, else the company's O'Fallon
  location, or its first.
- **The due date**: the bill's date and the vendor's terms in NetSuite,
  else the due date on the bill. One picked by hand stays.
- **Each line's account**: a line like one on the vendor's earlier bills
  (half its words or more) goes where that one went, and the biggest line
  where their bills keep going (the same account on each of their latest
  bills, or on two thirds of up to twelve; a bill whose expense lines come
  to under half of it, such as a PO's bill, doesn't count). With no bills
  yet, it's the vendor's default expense account in NetSuite. Anything else
  waits for someone to pick it, in amber. Only accounts the company can use
  are suggested.

The vendor's earlier bills are what the copy of NetSuite's bills has
(`gl_vendor_bill_history`): their latest 24, voided ones left out, each
expense line's account and memo (tax and item lines left out), and the
companies and locations the copy of the general ledger books each to.

Each change on the page asks the route again (`check`, a moment after the
typing stops), which works out the suggestions again for what hasn't been
picked by hand. The page takes the answer only if nothing changed
meanwhile.

### Before Send

Under the lines, green first, then amber, then red (`viewSnapForm`):

- whether the lines add up to the bill's total, which they must;
- when the bill says more is due than its own charges, that it likely
  carries an earlier balance, which isn't entered;
- **Already in NetSuite**: NetSuite has a bill from the vendor with that
  number (`findVendorBill`, asked as typed), or the copy of NetSuite's bills
  has one of theirs numbered the same in its letters and digits, whatever
  its date. Send stays greyed out, and the card links to NetSuite's bill;
- a bill, check or card charge to the vendor for the same amount within a
  week of the bill's date (`gl_mistake_docs`), which may be this bill
  entered already. It doesn't stop Send;
- what must change before Send: the vendor picked from NetSuite's list, a
  bill number (up to 40 characters), the bill's date, a due date on or
  after it, one of the company's locations, a total over $0, and each
  line's amount and an account the company can use. A company not linked
  to a NetSuite subsidiary can't send.

Send to NetSuite saves the form first, so a refusal loses nothing, then
checks it all again on the server, asking NetSuite again. It claims the
snapped bill (`makeBillOnce`, as Create Bill does a vendor invoice), asks
NetSuite once more for a bill from the vendor with that number, and makes
the bill (`createVendorBill`): the vendor, the bill number as its Reference
No., the company's subsidiary, the location, the bill's date, the due date
(left out when there's none, so NetSuite works it out from the terms), the
memo "FleetSuite Snap a bill", and each line to its account for its amount,
its description as its memo. When NetSuite doesn't say which bill it made
(the connection drops, or no id comes back), FleetSuite asks it for the
bill before calling the Send failed. Then it marks the snapped bill sent
with NetSuite's id for the bill, keeping the form that went, and Books keeps
its own copy (`saveOwnBill`, made from `snapped`). What NetSuite says when it
refuses is kept on the snapped bill and shown in red. When NetSuite made the
bill but FleetSuite couldn't mark it sent, the claim stays, so nobody sends
it again while it reads as a draft, and the page says to find the bill in
NetSuite. Once the claim has gone stale (15 minutes), the Already in
NetSuite card of a bill whose Send didn't finish offers Mark it sent: it
asks NetSuite for the bill and keeps it as sent, with Books' own copy,
without sending anything.

### The page

Save for later keeps the form as it is, and the start screen lists the
bills saved for later. Nothing on a bill can change while it's being read,
saved or sent, and no Save goes onto a bill a Send holds. Throw it away
asks once more. A bill sent shows
NetSuite's bill, who sent it and Snap another. Own bills lists it with who
sent it ("Snapped by"), linking back here.

### What isn't done yet

- The picture isn't attached to NetSuite's bill; it stays with Books'.
- Vendor credits and paying bills aren't part of it.

## PO bills

From the day part 29 shipped, a snapped bill that names one of the vendor's
open purchase orders bills that PO in NetSuite, the way Parts Mail's Create
bill does (owner decision 2026-10-25, "Freight line", as Books Previews drew
it; `src/lib/books/snap-po.ts`, migration 444). NetSuite bills the PO's open
lines at its prices, which settles what Receiving booked for them, and the
PO shows billed. What the vendor's bill has beyond what's left on the PO,
freight say, goes on the same bill as its own line, to an account picked for
it. When that's only lines the bill prints that aren't on the PO, it needs
no reason (owner decision 2026-10-26, "No reason for freight").

### The PO

The reader reads the PO number off the bill, and the PO box under the due
date takes one typed. The PO is looked for in FleetSuite's copy of
NetSuite's POs (`netsuite_vendor_pos`, every two hours) by its number, case
aside, else the vendor's newest with the same digits ("453" or "P.O. #453"
for PO453-ACME), else the one PO anyone has with those digits (`findPo`). A
bill that names none is offered up to five of the vendor's POs that can take
a bill, to tap: one for exactly the bill's total first, then those received,
partly received and still to come, newest first in each (`pickOpenPos`).

A PO can take the bill when it's the vendor's, NetSuite bills it (not
pending approval, rejected, planned, fully billed or closed:
`canBillPoStatus`; a Pending Bill PO, fully received, can, which Parts
Mail's check used to show in red) and something on it is left to bill.
Without a vendor picked yet, the PO's vendor is offered. FleetSuite's copy
of a PO keeps no company, so a PO's bill is BMG's, and the page hides the
company and location while it bills the PO: the PO brings them.

### The checks

Bill PO453-ACME, the default once the PO can take the bill, shows what
NetSuite bills (the PO's open lines, each line's quantity less what's
billed at its price, and the extra line) and Parts Mail's three-way match
(`computeThreeWayMatch` in `src/lib/three-way-match.ts`), each leg on its
own:

- **Totals**: the bill's total against the PO's, green within $25 or 2%,
  whichever is more;
- **Received**: how much of what's ordered Receiving has had, the short
  lines named; billing the PO bills all of it;
- **Earlier bills**: Parts Mail's and snapped bills already on the PO, and
  the units NetSuite shows billed on it.

A bill beyond the PO only by lines it prints that aren't on the PO, each
saying what it is (freight, say), has its totals checked without them, so
it bills with one tap like a bill for the PO's total: "The bill is the PO's
$2,140.00 plus $240.00 for freight, which goes on its own line." That's
only for a PO with nothing billed on it yet. Leaving the extra line off, a
price that changed as well as the freight, a line that doesn't say what it
is, or more of one of the PO's items keeps the totals check as it is.

A check that didn't pass needs a reason (Why bill it anyway?), and Bill it
anyway logs it as Parts Mail's Create bill does
(`vendor_bill_variance_override`, which the weekly digest reads). Plain bill
enters the bill's own lines, as Snap a bill otherwise does, and says it
leaves the PO open, with what Receiving booked for it unsettled.

### The extra line

The extra line is what the bill is more than what's left to bill on the PO:
its total less what's billed on its lines at its prices (`poLeftCents`), so
a second bill on a PO is set against what's left. Its words come from the
bill's own lines that aren't on the PO (not for one of its lines' amounts,
nor naming one of its items): the one for exactly that amount, else all of
them when they add up to it (`linesBeyondPo`, `extraWords`), else they're
typed. When each of them says what it is, the totals check leaves them out
(The checks). Its account is suggested only from a line like it on the
vendor's earlier bills (`suggestExtraAccount`), never where their bills'
parts go, and must be one BMG can use. Leave it off bills the PO alone, and
the page says what's left off; Add it back puts it back.

### Send

Send checks it all again on the server, claims the snapped bill and asks
NetSuite once more for a bill from the vendor with that number, as Snap a
bill always does. Then it bills the PO (`createBillFromPo`, the transform
Parts Mail's Create bill uses) with the bill number as its Reference No.,
the bill's date, the due date, the memo and the extra line as an expense
line after the PO's. The snapped bill is marked sent with the PO it billed
(`billed_po_netsuite_id`, `billed_po_number`) and the extra line's amount
(`billed_extra`), and Books keeps its own copy: the PO's open lines, as a
Parts Mail bill's, then the extra line. Until FleetSuite's copy of the PO
shows it billed, a snapped bill that billed a PO counts as an earlier bill
on it, for Parts Mail's Create bill too.

### What isn't done yet

- FleetSuite's copy of a PO keeps only its item lines, so a PO's other
  lines aren't shown, and Books' own copy differs from NetSuite's by them.
- The totals check, as Parts Mail's, compares a second bill on a PO with
  the PO's whole total, so it needs a reason.
- Mark it sent, once FleetSuite's copy shows the PO billed, keeps the bill
  as a plain one.

## Mistake checks

Each night FleetSuite looks through the bills, checks and card charges it
copies from NetSuite for likely mistakes, and lists each one, with why, on
Books → Mistake checks for the bookkeeper or a super admin to clear (owner
decision 2026-10-24, "Mistake checks": an idea borrowed from bookkeeping
software, built next to the crossover, for the owner alone). Nothing is
written to NetSuite or to the books: a mistake is fixed in NetSuite, and the
copies bring the fix back.

`/api/cron/books-mistake-checks` runs at 09:46 UTC (4:46 AM Central, 3:46
in winter), after the night's copies of NetSuite's checks and card charges
(:33 on odd hours) and bills (:39), and before the daily check (:58), which
tells the super admins about new ones ("Daily check"). Look now on the page
runs the same look straight away. The look (`runMistakeChecks` in
`src/lib/books/mistake-checks.ts`) reads the copies a quarter at a time
(`gl_mistake_docs`, migration 431) and keeps what it finds at once
(`gl_mistake_checks_save`); the record in `sync_state`,
`books_mistake_checks`, holds how it went.

It flags documents dated from September 1, 2026 (`LOOK_FROM`), and works
out what's usual for a vendor from everything the copies hold, since
January 2025 (`USUAL_FROM`). It looks at the bills, checks and card charges
NetSuite still has, not voided, with a vendor or payee; bill credits and
card refunds aren't flagged. A document's company is the one its GL lines
post to; one the copy of NetSuite's ledger hasn't reached yet goes with the
company the vendor's others post to most, or BMG.

### The four checks

- **Entered twice.** Two bills from the same vendor for the same amount
  with the same invoice number (its letters and digits, in any case), the
  likeliest; a bill and a check or card charge to the same vendor for the
  same amount within 7 days, which may be one bill entered both ways; and
  two checks or card charges to the same payee for the same amount on the
  same day, from $25, when there are just the two. Each pair is one item
  (`twice:<id>+<id>`), flagged when the later of the two is dated from
  September 1.
- **Far off usual.** A document from a vendor with at least six earlier
  ones: when their last six to twelve were all within 5% of each other and
  this one is ten times that or a tenth of it, the larger $100 or more,
  which can be a slipped decimal point; otherwise when it's $500 or more
  and over twice the most they've billed or charged (`far_off:<id>`).
- **Monthly charge missing.** A vendor that billed or charged a company
  every month for at least six months in a row, with nothing for the month
  a week after the day it usually comes (the middle of the days each
  month's first came on). The month just past is looked at until the
  current month's would be late. Its amount is about what the months came
  to (`missing:<company>:<vendor>:<YYYY-MM>`). It stays open until
  something comes for that month, even when later months break the run.
- **Odd account.** A document coded to an expense account none of the
  vendor's earlier documents used, when there are at least five with
  accounts and they used no more than three between them
  (`odd_account:<id>`). Tax lines and item lines aren't looked at: an
  item's account comes with the item.

### Clearing

Each item (`gl_mistake_checks`) is open until it's cleared:

- **Fixed in NetSuite**, by the bookkeeper or a super admin, with a note if
  they like. If the look still finds it once the copies made after the clear
  have it (the older of the bills' and the checks and card charges' last
  runs), it comes back, marked "Back" with the day.
- **Fine as it is**, by the bookkeeper or a super admin, with a note. For an
  odd account or a monthly charge a box, unticked to start, also teaches the
  check: those accounts are fine for that vendor from now on, or that
  monthly charge isn't watched. Opening the item again unlearns it.
- **On its own**, when the copies no longer show it: a document gone from
  NetSuite, voided, or changed so the two no longer match, the missing
  charge come in, or an account now fine for the vendor. It says what
  changed, and comes back if the look finds it again.

The bookkeeper or a super admin can open any cleared item again
(`gl_mistake_check_reopen`). Who cleared each one, when, how and with what
note is kept.

### The page

`/admin/books/mistake-checks` (`deepLinks.booksMistakeChecks`,
`?company=`, `&item=` to pick one out) shows a company's open items, the
likeliest first, or its cleared ones, newest first, each with why, its
documents linked to NetSuite and the buttons to clear it; when the look
and the copies it looked through last ran, and anything that failed, in
red; and Look now. Books links to it, and the daily check's notifications
open it.

### What isn't done yet

- Bill credits, card refunds and journal entries aren't looked at.
- Amounts are compared to the cent, so a bill entered twice with a typo in
  its amount isn't caught.
- Only expense lines' accounts are looked at, not items'.

## Credit memos made in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 staff make credit memos in FleetSuite, and FleetSuite
makes each one in NetSuite and keeps Books' own copy (the NetSuite Exit
Checklist's January pieces, owner decisions 2026-10-31). It's built
switched off ("Switches"): until a super admin turns it on, only super
admins can open the screen, to see what it would send, and nothing goes to
NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with Invoicing can.

On Invoicing → Invoiced each NetSuite invoice has a Credit button, which
opens `/invoices/credit-memo?invoice=<NetSuite id>`
(`deepLinks.creditMemo`). The screen reads the invoice from NetSuite as it
is now (`getInvoiceRecord`), with the credit memos FleetSuite already made
against it and any others NetSuite has (`getInvoiceCreditMemos`, through
the transaction line links). Then the person picks what to credit
(`workOutCreditMemo` in `src/lib/credit-memo.ts`):

- **A line, by quantity and price each**, never more than the invoice
  billed or charged each, nor more money than is left of the line after
  earlier credit memos. Credit it all fills in what's left of every line.
- **A discount or markup**, or a line that took money off such as a
  trade-in, by its amount. A discount credited back takes money off the
  credit.
- **An amount of its own**, such as goodwill, with what it's for. It goes
  on the credit item, and gives back sales tax only when ticked and the
  invoice charged tax.
- **Why**, which goes on the credit memo as its memo.

A stock part credited on its own item goes back on the shelf, as NetSuite
does with any credit memo, booked to O'Fallon, the only location that keeps
stock, even when the invoice is from somewhere else. With "Back to stock"
off (a price fix on a part that stays on the truck, say), the money goes on
the NetSuite Credit Item instead, which a super admin picks in Settings: a
Non-inventory, Other charge or Service item, so nothing comes back on the
shelf (`src/lib/credit-item.ts`, `quote_settings.netsuite_credit_item_id`).
Until one is picked, only parts coming back can be credited. Labor and
other items NetSuite keeps no stock of are always credited on their own
item.

Each line is taxed as the invoice taxed it, at the invoice's own rate:
NetSuite's rate on the invoice, else its tax code's from the copy of
NetSuite's lists, else what its tax comes to over its taxed lines. Each
line's tax is rounded half to even, as Books' own invoices are
(`credit_tax@1`). An invoice that charged no tax gives none back.

Make credit memo sends it once. The page makes an id when it opens, and the
route (`/api/invoices/credit-memo`) works the credit memo out again from the
invoice as NetSuite has it now, never from what the page sent: when that
comes to a different total than the page showed, nothing is sent and the
page says to reload. It takes a claim on the press (`invoice_credit_memos`,
as typed invoices do), makes the credit memo with NetSuite's own Credit on
the invoice (`createCreditMemoFromInvoice`: the invoice → credit memo
transform with `?replace=item`, so the credit memo has exactly the lines
picked), and keeps NetSuite's id and number. Pressing again says what the
first press made; a refusal lets go of the claim and shows NetSuite's
reason in red, so the same form can try again. A press still being made
counts against what's left, so two people can't credit the same thing
twice. NetSuite applies the credit memo to the invoice while it's still
owed; anything more stays as the customer's credit. Nothing is emailed to
the customer. The PDF opens from the screen once it's made.

Books keeps its own copy of each one, worked out by these rules rather than
read back from NetSuite (`gl_own_credit_memos` and their lines, saved only
by `gl_own_credit_memo_save`, `src/lib/books/own-credit-memos.ts`): each
line with the invoice line it credits, its tax, and whether the part came
back on the shelf or the money went on the credit item. Saving it never
stops a credit memo; one that can't be saved is said on the screen in red.

Made-up example: an invoice bills 2 shelf units at $125.50, taxed at 8%,
and 3 hours of labor at $95.00. One unit comes back: crediting 1 at $125.50
with Back to stock on gives back $125.50 and $10.04 of tax, $135.54, and
puts the unit back on the shelf at O'Fallon.

### What isn't done yet

- Books' own copies aren't checked against NetSuite's credit memos, listed
  or posted yet. Until they are, NetSuite's credit memo posts from the copy
  of NetSuite's ledger as any credit memo does ("Credit memos").
- Before switching on, check the first few in NetSuite: a credit memo with
  a discount line, one with a part coming back to O'Fallon from an invoice
  booked elsewhere, and how NetSuite applied it to the invoice.
- A credit memo with no invoice behind it isn't made in FleetSuite.

## Vendor credits made in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 admins enter vendors' credits in FleetSuite, and
FleetSuite makes each one in NetSuite, applies it to the vendor's open
bills, and keeps Books' own copy (the NetSuite Exit Checklist's January
pieces, owner decisions 2026-10-31). It's built switched off
("Switches"): until a super admin turns it on, only super admins can open
the screen, to see what it would send, and nothing goes to NetSuite. Once
it's on, admins with Parts Ordering can, and More → Purchasing shows
Vendor Credits to them.

The screen is `/admin/purchasing/vendor-credit` (`deepLinks.vendorCredit`).
The person picks the company (BMG unless they pick another), finds the
vendor in the copy of NetSuite's vendors, and enters the vendor's own
number for the credit, the date on it, the location and why the vendor
gave it, which goes on the vendor credit as its memo. Then what it's for
(`workOutVendorCredit` in `src/lib/vendor-credit.ts`):

- **Money back to an account**: one of the accounts the company can put a
  bill's line on (Snap a bill's list), with an amount and, if wanted, what
  it's for. A new line starts on the vendor's usual expense account.
- **A part going back to the vendor**, from FleetSuite's copy of NetSuite's
  active items, by quantity and what the vendor credits for each, which
  starts at what BMG pays for it. A line is its quantity times that, rounded
  half to even to the cent (`vendor_credit@1`). Parts go back only on BMG's
  vendor credits. A stock part comes off the shelf, so a credit sending one
  back is booked at O'Fallon, the only location that keeps stock; the screen
  says when FleetSuite shows fewer on hand than are going back.

There's no sales tax on a vendor credit, as there's none on a bill. The
screen reads the vendor's bills NetSuite has open now for the company
(`getVendorOpenBills`, with the credits already open on the vendor shown
under them so the same one isn't entered twice). The person ticks the
bills to apply it to, with how much on each, or presses Apply oldest first
(`applyOldestFirst`), which goes oldest bill first as far as the credit
goes, as NetSuite's Auto Apply would. Nothing more than a bill has open,
nor more than the credit comes to, can be applied. What isn't applied stays
open on the vendor, for NetSuite to apply when the vendor is next paid.

Make vendor credit sends it once. The page makes an id when it opens, and
the route (`/api/purchasing/vendor-credit`) works the credit out again from
the copies and from the vendor's bills as NetSuite has them now, never from
what the page sent: when that comes to a different total than the page
showed, nothing is sent and the page says to reload. It asks NetSuite
whether it already has a vendor credit from that vendor under that number
(`findVendorCredit`), and sends nothing when it does or when NetSuite can't
say. It takes a claim on the press (`typed_vendor_credits`), and one press
being made or made holds the vendor's number, so two screens can't enter the
same credit twice. Then it makes the vendor credit (`createVendorCredit`:
the account lines as expenses, the parts as items with the amount worked
out, the location on the header as a bill's is, in the vendor's currency,
and the bills picked on its apply list) and keeps NetSuite's id. Pressing
again says what the first press made; a refusal lets go of the claim and
shows NetSuite's reason in red, so the same form can try again. Nothing is
emailed to the vendor. The screen lists the vendor credits entered lately,
with any NetSuite refused.

Books keeps its own copy of each one, worked out by these rules rather than
read back from NetSuite (`gl_own_vendor_credits` and their lines, saved only
by `gl_own_vendor_credit_save`, `src/lib/books/own-vendor-credits.ts`):
each line's account or part, quantity and rate, the bills it was applied to
and how much went on each. Its lines must add up to its total and its bills
to what it applied. Saving it never stops a vendor credit; one that can't
be saved is said on the screen in red.

Made-up example: a vendor takes back a shelf unit BMG paid $125.50 for and
refunds a $20.00 restocking fee. One part line, 1 at $125.50, and one
account line, $20.00 to the shop supplies account, make a $145.50 credit.
Applied to the vendor's oldest open bill, which has $50.00 left on it, $50.00
goes there and $95.50 stays open on the vendor; the unit comes off the
shelf at O'Fallon.

### What isn't done yet

- Books' own copies aren't checked against NetSuite's vendor credits (the
  copy of NetSuite's bills, `gl_netsuite_bills`, has them as `VendCred`),
  listed or posted yet. Until they are, NetSuite's vendor credit posts from
  the copy of NetSuite's ledger as any vendor credit does.
- Before switching on, check the first few in NetSuite: one applied to two
  bills (that NetSuite's apply list took each amount as sent), one with a
  part going back (that the line kept the amount sent with its quantity and
  rate, and the part left O'Fallon's stock), and one left unapplied.
- A vendor credit is filled in from a bill only to undo all of it (Credit
  all of it, "Bills fixed in FleetSuite"); one for a return of parts
  received on a PO is entered on its own.

## Card charges coded in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 the Chase card's charges are coded in FleetSuite, and
FleetSuite sends each month's charges to NetSuite as one journal entry and
keeps Books' own copy (the NetSuite Exit Checklist's January pieces, owner
decisions 2026-10-31). The bookkeeper enters the card's statements in
NetSuite as journal entries (owner, 2026-10-16: NetSuite has no credit card
charges of BMG's), so FleetSuite does the same. It's built switched off
("Switches"): until a super admin turns it on, only super admins can open
the screen, to add and code charges and see what a send would make, and
nothing goes to NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with Payments can, and More
→ Money & Books shows Card Charges to them.

The screen is `/admin/card-charges` (`deepLinks.cardCharges`), and it needs
price access too. The person picks the company (BMG unless they pick
another) and the card's own account in the chart (a credit card or other
current liability account the company can use), and adds the card's
activity as Chase downloads it: in Chase, open the card, choose Download
account activity, and pick CSV. `readChaseDownload` in
`src/lib/card-charges.ts` finds the columns by their headings (Transaction
Date, Post Date, Description, Category, Type, Amount and Memo, with Card
first when the account has more than one card) and refuses the whole file
when any line can't be read, saying which. Chase shows a purchase as money
out, below 0; a file whose sales are all above 0 is read the other way
round, and one whose sales go both ways is refused. Payments to the card
are left out and counted, since they're paid from the bank, which stays in
NetSuite. So are $0 lines.

Each charge is kept once per card however often a download repeats it
(`card_charges`, by its fingerprint: its dates, description, amount and
card; two identical charges in one file are told apart by their place). The
merchant is the description's words up to the first that's a store number
or a reference (`merchantKey`), so "ACME HARDWARE #1234" and "ACME HARDWARE
#5678" are one merchant. Saving charges' accounts remembers each merchant's
for the company (`card_merchant_rules`: the account most of its charges
went to in that save; the latest save wins), and a new charge from a
remembered merchant comes in coded to it, marked Remembered until a person
saves it. Picking an account on one charge fills it in on the merchant's
other uncoded charges on the screen. A charge can have a note, which goes
on its lines in NetSuite. Undo on a file takes its charges back off, all
but those sent or being sent.

The screen shows the charges by month, oldest first, with how many are left
to code. Send sends one month as one journal entry (`workOutCardEntry`,
`card_charges@1`): for each charge, a line to its account and a line to the
card's account, to the cent, so the card's side matches Chase's statement
line for line. A charge is a debit to its account and a credit to the card,
its balance owed; money back from a merchant goes the other way. The entry
is dated the month's latest charge, and its memo names the card's account
and the month. Every charge in the month has to be coded first, to an
account the company can use that isn't the card's own. One journal entry
takes 250 charges at most, so a busier month goes in parts, oldest first.
No month before January 2027 can be sent: those are on the bookkeeper's
journal entries in NetSuite already.

Send sends it once. The page makes an id for each month's press, and the
route (`/api/card-charges`) claims the month's coded charges for it
(`card_charges_claim`, `card_charge_sends`) and works the entry out again
from the charges as they're kept, never from what the page sent: when they
come to a different count or amount than the page showed, it lets go of
them and nothing is sent. The press's id is the journal entry's External ID
in NetSuite (`fleetsuite-card-` and the id). The route asks NetSuite for it
first (`findJournalEntry`) and sends nothing when NetSuite can't say. Then
it makes the journal entry (`createJournalEntry`, which refuses one whose
debits and credits don't match to the cent) and keeps NetSuite's id on the
press. Pressing again says what the first press made. A refusal lets go of
the charges and shows NetSuite's reason in red. When NetSuite may have made
it anyway (no answer, a server error, or no id in its answer), the charges
stay held; pressing again after five minutes asks NetSuite, and the send
finishes or lets go. A held charge can't be coded or taken back off.

Books keeps its own copy of each journal entry, worked out by these rules
rather than read back from NetSuite (`gl_own_journal_entries` and their
lines, saved only by `gl_own_journal_entry_save`,
`src/lib/books/own-journal-entries.ts`): each line's account, debit or
credit, memo and the charge it's for. Its debits must match its credits and
its total. Saving it never stops a send; one that can't be saved is said on
the screen in red.

Made-up example: in January a card has a $125.50 hardware purchase on the
3rd, then $20.00 back from the same store and a $42.10 marketplace order on
the 7th, coded to shop supplies, shop supplies and freight in. They make one
journal entry of six lines dated January 7: shop supplies debited $125.50 and credited $20.00,
freight in debited $42.10, and the card's account credited $125.50 and
$42.10 and debited $20.00, $147.60 onto the card in all.

### What isn't done yet

- Books' own copies aren't checked against NetSuite's journal entries,
  listed or posted yet. Until they are, the journal entry posts from the
  copy of NetSuite's ledger as the bookkeeper's do.
- Before switching on, check against a real Chase download and NetSuite:
  that the download's headings and signs are as read here (and its Card
  column, when the account has more than one card); that NetSuite's query
  finds a journal entry by its External ID and refuses a second under the
  same one; whether journal entry approval holds FleetSuite's for approval;
  whether a line needs a location, department or class; and what NetSuite
  says to a closed month. Then send the first month and compare it with
  Chase's statement.
- Payments to the card aren't matched to the bank's; they stay in NetSuite
  with banking.

## Journal entries typed in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 the journal entries the bookkeeper types into NetSuite
today (accruals, reclasses, depreciation and the like) are typed in
FleetSuite, approved, and sent on to NetSuite, and Books keeps its own copy
(the NetSuite Exit Checklist's January pieces, owner decisions
2026-10-31). It's built switched off ("Switches"): until a super admin
turns it on, only super admins can open the screen, to type and save
entries and see what they'd make, and none is asked for approval or sent
to NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with Payments can, and More → Money &
Books shows Journal Entries to them.

The screen is `/admin/journal-entries` (`deepLinks.journalEntries`, with
the entry's id to open one), and it needs price access too. An entry has a
company (BMG unless they pick another), a date, a memo saying what it's
for, and its lines: each an account in the chart the company can use, a
debit or a credit, and a note if wanted. The totals show as it's typed, in
green when they balance and in red with how far apart when they don't.
Save keeps it as a draft (`typed_journal_entries`, saved only by
`typed_journal_entry_save`), whole or half typed. Only a draft can be
changed, and only whoever started it or a super admin can delete it.

Ask for approval checks it first (`workOutTypedEntry` in
`src/lib/typed-journal-entries.ts`, `typed_journal_entry@1`): the company
needs a NetSuite subsidiary; the entry needs a real date, a memo, and two
lines or more, 200 at most; each line needs an account the company can
use and a debit or a credit above $0, not both, to the cent and
$100,000,000.00 at most; and its debits must match its credits. A line to
Accounts Receivable or Accounts Payable isn't taken: NetSuite needs a
customer or vendor there, so those are an invoice, credit memo, bill or
vendor credit instead. Any date is taken, so a year-end entry dated
December 31 is fine. The ask names the draft as the asker saw it
(`typed_journal_entry_ask`): saved again since, it isn't asked. Every super
admin but the asker hears it's waiting (`books_journal_entry_waiting`), and
so does anyone with "Copy me on new alerts" ticked, by email too.

A waiting entry can't be changed. Whoever asked, whoever started it, or a
super admin can take it back to a draft (`typed_journal_entry_take_back`).
A super admin can send it back with why (`typed_journal_entry_send_back`),
shown in red on the draft until it's asked again, and whoever asked hears
it (`books_journal_entry_decided`).

A super admin who didn't ask approves it, and FleetSuite makes it in
NetSuite once, exactly as typed. The approval names the ask the approver
saw (`typed_journal_entry_claim`): taken back or asked again since, nothing
is sent. The route (`/api/journal-entries`) works the entry out again from
its lines as they're kept, never from what the page sent, and lets go when
they no longer make one. The entry's id, made by the page when it's
started, is the journal entry's External ID in NetSuite (`fleetsuite-je-`
and the id). The route asks NetSuite for it first (`findJournalEntry`) and
sends nothing when NetSuite can't say. Then it makes it
(`createJournalEntry`) and keeps NetSuite's id on the entry
(`typed_journal_entry_made`). Pressing again says what it made. A refusal
puts it back to waiting for approval, with NetSuite's reason in red
(`typed_journal_entry_let_go`). When NetSuite may have made it anyway, it
stays approved; Ask NetSuite after five minutes finds out, and it finishes
or goes back to waiting. Whoever asked hears it's made. A made entry can
be copied into a new draft, or reversed into one: each debit a credit and
each credit a debit, dated the first of the next month.

Books keeps its own copy of each one made (`gl_own_journal_entries`,
source `typed`, `ownTypedEntryPayload` in
`src/lib/books/own-journal-entries.ts`): each line's account, debit or
credit and note, as typed. Saving it never stops an approval; one that
can't be saved is said on the screen in red.

Made-up example: at the end of January, depreciation of $1,250.00 is
typed as three lines: depreciation debited $800.00 for the shop truck and
$450.00 for the box van, and accumulated depreciation credited $1,250.00.
Asked for approval by someone with Payments and approved by a super
admin, it's one journal entry of three lines in NetSuite, dated January 31.

### The CPA's adjustments journal

Part 53 (migration 484) gives the CPA a journal of its own, finishing the
NetSuite Exit Checklist's "Bookkeeper and CPA access": the CPA reads every
Books page and changes nothing there ("Who can open the books"), and types
its adjustments (depreciation, loan interest, the year-end entries) on this
screen, in its own journal and no other. Each entry is in one of two
(`typed_journal_entries.journal`):

| Journal | Who types in it, and sees it | Its memo in NetSuite and in Books' copy |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `general` | Anyone with Payments, as before part 53 | As typed |
| `cpa_adjustments` | The CPA, and super admins for them | As typed, starting "CPA adjustment: " |

`journalsFor` in `src/lib/typed-journal-entries.ts` says who types in
which, and the route checks it on every step by the caller's own roles: the
list holds only the caller's journals, and saving, deleting, asking, taking
back, sending back or approving an entry in another one answers 403. A
super admin has both and picks the journal when starting an entry; the
CPA's screen is CPA adjustments alone. An entry stays in the journal it was
started in (`typed_journal_entry_save` answers `other_journal` and changes
nothing), and a copy or reversal of one starts in its journal. Every entry
typed before part 53 is in the general journal.

A CPA adjustment is checked, asked for, approved and made as any other: a
super admin who didn't ask approves it, and the CPA approves nothing. Only
its memo differs: NetSuite and Books' own copy show it starting "CPA
adjustment: " (`journalMemo`, and not twice when it's typed that way), so
the memo takes 284 characters, NetSuite's 300 less those. Its alerts call it
a CPA adjustment. One dated in a month closed in FleetSuite's books is still
made in NetSuite, and posts there once a super admin reopens the month, as
any entry does ("Closing a month").

It rides the `journal_entries` switch: while that's off, only super admins
type either journal's entries, and the CPA, like anyone else, reads that
journal entries are switched off. Once it's on, Books → CPA adjustments
opens the CPA's screen, and View As → CPA shows it. The bookkeeper is
unchanged: stack Finance on them for the general journal.

Made-up example: on December 31, 2026 the CPA types the year's
depreciation, $9,600.00 to depreciation against accumulated depreciation,
with the memo "2026 depreciation". A super admin approves it, and NetSuite
has one journal entry of two lines whose memo is "CPA adjustment: 2026
depreciation".

### What isn't done yet

- Books' own copies aren't checked against NetSuite's journal entries,
  listed or posted yet. Until they are, the journal entry posts from the
  copy of NetSuite's ledger as the bookkeeper's do.
- A super admin's own entries wait for another super admin to approve
  them, so approving needs two super admins.
- A line can't name a customer, vendor or employee (NetSuite's Name
  column), which is why Accounts Receivable and Payable lines are refused.
- Entries that repeat each month, like depreciation, are copied by hand:
  nothing makes them on a schedule yet.
- Before switching on, check against NetSuite: that NetSuite's own journal
  entry approval doesn't hold FleetSuite's for approval a second time;
  whether a line needs a location, department or class; what NetSuite says
  to a closed month; and that the accounts offered are the ones the
  bookkeeper uses. Then type the first month-end entry here instead of in
  NetSuite and compare.

## Inventory transfers made in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 stock moved from one of a company's locations to
another is moved in FleetSuite, and FleetSuite makes NetSuite's Transfer
Inventory for it (the NetSuite Exit Checklist's January pieces, owner
decisions 2026-10-31). It's built switched off ("Switches"): until a super
admin turns it on, only super admins can open the screen, to make and save
transfers and see what they'd send, and nothing is moved in NetSuite. Once
it's on, admins can, as they can use Inventory Adjust, and More → Parts &
Inventory shows Inventory Transfers to them. Nothing about a transfer
alerts anyone, as Inventory Adjust doesn't.

The screen is `/admin/inventory-transfers` (`deepLinks.inventoryTransfers`,
with the transfer's id to open one). A transfer has a company (BMG unless
they pick another), the location the stock is at (O'Fallon to start with)
and the one it's going to, both the company's in NetSuite, a date, a memo
saying why it's moving, and its items. Each item is found by its number or
name among the active parts NetSuite keeps stock of: inventory and
assembly items, and a part whose kind the parts list doesn't say, as
Inventory Adjust takes it. Each says how many move, to four places. Beside
each is NetSuite's count of it at both locations, as Books' stock copy last
read it (migration 401), with a warning when more is moving than was
counted where it's moving from. Save keeps it as a draft
(`inventory_transfers`, saved only by `inventory_transfer_save`), whole or
half typed. Only a draft can be changed, and only whoever started it or a
super admin can delete it.

Move checks it first (`workOutTransfer` in `src/lib/inventory-transfers.ts`,
`inventory_transfer@1`): the company needs a NetSuite subsidiary; the
transfer needs a real date, a memo, two different locations of the
company's, and one item or more, 100 at most; each item must be active,
kept stock of, on one line only, and moving more than 0 and 1,000,000 at
most, to four places. The press names the draft as the mover saw it
(`inventory_transfer_claim`): saved again since, nothing moves. The route
(`/api/inventory-transfers`) works the transfer out again from its lines as
they're kept, never from what the page sent, and lets go when they no
longer make one. The transfer's id, made by the page when it's started, is
its External ID in NetSuite (`fleetsuite-it-` and the id). The route asks
NetSuite for it first (`findInventoryTransfer`) and moves nothing when
NetSuite can't say. Then it reads NetSuite's count now at the location it's
moving from (`getLocationOnHand`) and moves nothing when an item has fewer
there than are moving, saying how many NetSuite has. Then it makes it
(`createInventoryTransfer`: the location it's moving from, the one it's
going to, and each item's quantity) and keeps NetSuite's id and number on
the transfer (`inventory_transfer_made`). Pressing again says what it made.
A refusal makes it a draft again, with NetSuite's reason in red
(`inventory_transfer_let_go`). When NetSuite may have made it anyway, it's
held; Ask NetSuite after five minutes finds out, and it finishes or goes
back to a draft. A made transfer can be copied into a new draft, or moved
back: the same items, from where they went to where they came from.

A transfer changes where stock is, never how much there is or what it's
worth: NetSuite moves it at the cost it carries it at. Books already copies
every NetSuite transfer (the stock copy, migration 401, and the GL copy),
so one made here is counted in Stock by item and posts from the GL copy as
any other does (`inventory_transfer` entries, "Stock and cost"), and there's
no own copy to keep.

Made-up example: four bracket kits and 12.5 feet of wire are moved from
O'Fallon to the service van, dated January 15, with the memo "Stock for the
van". NetSuite has 10 kits and 250.5 feet at O'Fallon, so it's one transfer
of two lines in NetSuite.

### What isn't done yet

- Stock moved between BMG and 1084 isn't a transfer: NetSuite needs a
  transfer order between the two subsidiaries, and nothing here makes one.
- An item NetSuite tracks by lot or serial number needs its numbers on the
  transfer (NetSuite's inventory detail), which the screen doesn't take, so
  NetSuite refuses it.
- Before switching on, check against NetSuite: that the screen offers the
  locations each company really moves stock between; whether a location
  uses bins; what NetSuite says to a closed month; and that NetSuite
  doesn't hold Transfer Inventory for approval. Then make the first real
  transfer here instead of in NetSuite and compare.

## Invoices fixed in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 staff fix an invoice after it's made in FleetSuite,
and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite and in Books' own copy of the
invoice (the NetSuite Exit Checklist's January pieces, owner decisions
2026-10-31). It's built switched off ("Switches"): until a super admin
turns it on, only super admins can open the screen, to see what a fix
would send, and nothing goes to NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with
Invoicing who can see prices can.

On Invoicing → Invoiced each NetSuite invoice has a Fix button beside
Credit, which opens `/invoices/fix?invoice=<NetSuite id>`
(`deepLinks.invoiceFix`). The screen reads the invoice from NetSuite as it
is now (`getInvoiceRecord`, `parseFixInvoice` in `src/lib/invoice-fix.ts`),
every line with it, the ones that only lay it out too. What a fix can
change (`fixAllows`):

- **Its PO number, memo and VIN**, whatever has been paid on it: 45 and
  999 characters at most, and a VIN is 17 letters and numbers with no I, O
  or Q.
- **While nothing on it is paid or credited, its date and its lines.** Its
  due date moves with its date by its terms: 30 days after on Net 30, the
  same day with no terms. Terms FleetSuite can't work out (due on a day of
  the month) leave NetSuite's due date, and the screen says so. A line's
  quantity, price each and description, a discount's or markup's amount, a
  line taken off, and a line added of any item a new invoice can bill,
  found with the same search as New invoice. A new quantity on a line
  NetSuite gave no price each for needs its price each too, or the line
  couldn't say what it comes to. A line that takes money off
  without being a discount (a trade-in, say), or names no item, can only be
  taken off. Subtotals, descriptions and item groups stay as they are.
- **Once money is on it**, part paid, paid, or credited by a credit memo
  FleetSuite made, only its PO number, memo and VIN: a credit memo takes
  money off instead. A voided invoice can't be fixed.
- **Undoing a whole invoice** isn't a fix: that's a credit memo for all of
  it, which the screen links to. NetSuite's Void isn't used, so the books
  keep what was billed and what was taken back.

Each line is changed by NetSuite's own key for it, as the sales order page
changes an order's lines, so a line billed off a sales order stays billed
off it. Only what changed is sent (`planInvoiceFix`). Taking a line off
sends every line the invoice keeps, each by its key, with `?replace=item`,
so NetSuite's lines become exactly those. A new price goes at the Custom
price level, as on the invoices FleetSuite makes. NetSuite works the sales
tax out again: a line kept keeps its tax box, and a line added is taxed by
FleetSuite's own rule (`src/lib/line-taxability.ts`): nothing for a
tax-exempt customer or work at Social Circle, and a discount as the line
above it. The screen shows the totals now and after the fix, the tax at
the invoice's own rate, as the credit memo screen reads it; NetSuite's can
differ by a cent or so. On an invoice that charged no tax, the screen
can't say what a taxed line added will be charged.

Fix invoice sends it once (`/api/invoices/fix`). The page sends back what
it read the invoice as (`invoiceFacts`, as a short signature): when
NetSuite's invoice has changed since, nothing is sent and the page says to
reload. The route works the fix out again from the invoice as NetSuite has
it now, never from what the page worked out, and takes a claim on the press
(`invoice_fixes`, `invoice_fix_claim`, migration 471): pressing again says
the first press is being sent or went in, and only one fix of an invoice is
sent at a time. Then it sends NetSuite the change (`patchInvoice`). A
refusal, in NetSuite's own words (a closed month, say), is kept as refused:
nothing changed, and the same form can try again. When NetSuite doesn't
answer or faults, it's kept as unsure and the page says to reload and look:
a fix that went in shows on the invoice. A send that never finished holds
the invoice five minutes at most, then counts as unsure. Each fix keeps who
made it, why, what changed in words, what NetSuite was sent, and NetSuite's
total after, listed under Fixed before on the screen and in the audit log
(`invoice_fixes`, `fix`). Nothing is emailed to the customer.

Books' own copy follows (`saveFixedOwnInvoice` in
`src/lib/books/own-invoices.ts`, `gl_own_invoice_fix`). When FleetSuite has
its own copy of the invoice (one it made, since part 23), the fix makes the
copy's next version from the invoice as fixed (`gl_own_invoices.version`,
`lines_from` 'fixed'): its date, due date, PO number, memo, VIN and lines,
taxed by FleetSuite's own rule at the copy's own rate. The version before is
kept in `gl_own_invoice_versions`, and a copy changed since the fix read it
isn't overwritten. The copy's check (Own invoices) compares it with
NetSuite's fixed invoice once the copy of NetSuite's ledger has it, and the
next posting run reposts the invoice when its entry no longer fits. Saving
it never stops a fix; one that can't be saved is said on the screen in red,
and the check shows the copy differing until it's put right. An invoice
made in NetSuite has no copy of FleetSuite's own, so only NetSuite's
changes.

Made-up example: an invoice bills 2 shelf units at $125.50, taxed at
8.488%, and 3 hours of labor at $95.00, $557.30, and nothing is paid on it.
Three were fitted: Fix, the shelf's quantity 2 → 3, why "Billed two, fitted
three". The screen shows the total going to about $693.46, NetSuite's
invoice and Books' copy both change, and the copy keeps its first version.

### What isn't done yet

- Its customer, location, terms or sales rep can't be changed: credit it
  all and invoice it again.
- Fixing a bill is part 40 ("Bills fixed in FleetSuite").
- Before switching on, try fixes on a test invoice of each kind, typed and
  billed off a sales order, and check in NetSuite: that it works the tax
  out again; that the sales order's billed quantities follow a line billed
  off it that changes or comes off, and that it takes a line added to an
  invoice off a sales order; that a closed month refuses a fix; and that
  its answer has `lastModifiedDate`, `dueDate` and each line's `orderLine`
  as the screen reads them.

## Bills fixed in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 admins fix a bill after it's made in FleetSuite, and
FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite and in Books' own copy of the bill
(the NetSuite Exit Checklist's January pieces, owner decisions 2026-10-31).
It's built switched off ("Switches"): until a super admin turns it on, only
super admins can open the screen, to see what a fix would send, and nothing
goes to NetSuite. Once it's on, admins with Parts Ordering who can see
prices can, and More → Purchasing shows Fix a Bill to them.

The screen is `/admin/purchasing/bill-fix` (`deepLinks.billFix`). It finds
a bill in the copy of NetSuite's bills by its number or vendor, the newest
first, or opens on one: Books → Own bills has Fix this bill on each bill's
page. It reads the bill from NetSuite as it is now (`getVendorBillRecord`,
every line of both its lists, expenses and items, the ones that only lay
it out too) and what SuiteQL says of its money (`getVendorBillAmounts`: its
total and what's still owed, which the record doesn't say), in
`parseFixBill` in `src/lib/bill-fix.ts`. What a fix can change
(`billFixAllows`):

- **Its number, memo and due date**, whatever has been paid on it. The
  number is the vendor's own (NetSuite's Reference No.), 40 characters at
  most, and the memo 999. A number another of the vendor's bills has in
  NetSuite is refused (`findVendorBill`), as Snap a bill refuses one, and
  when NetSuite can't be asked nothing is sent.
- **While nothing on it is paid or credited, its date and its lines.** Its
  due date moves with its date by its terms: 30 days after on Net 30, the
  same day with no terms. Terms FleetSuite can't work out (due on a day of
  the month) leave NetSuite's due date, and the screen says so. An expense
  line's account (one the bill's company can put a bill's line on, Snap a
  bill's list), amount and memo; a part's quantity, rate and description; a
  line taken off; and an expense line added. A part's line comes to its
  quantity times its rate, rounded half to even to the cent, and NetSuite is
  sent that amount with them. A new quantity on a part NetSuite gave no rate
  for needs its rate too. A line that takes money off (a discount), or names
  no account or item, can only be taken off. Subtotals, descriptions and
  item groups stay as they are. A part isn't added by a fix: it's billed off
  its purchase order.
- **Once money is on it**, part paid, paid, or credited by a vendor credit
  FleetSuite made, only its number, memo and due date: a vendor credit takes
  money off instead. The same when NetSuite can't say what's still owed on
  it, or its lines don't come to its total. A voided bill can't be fixed.
- **Undoing a whole bill** isn't a fix: that's a vendor credit for all of
  it, Credit all of it on the screen (below). NetSuite's Void isn't used,
  so the books keep what was billed and what was taken back.

Each line is changed by NetSuite's own key for it on its list, as an
invoice's are, so a part billed off a purchase order stays billed off it,
and NetSuite moves the order's billed quantity with it. Only what changed
is sent (`planBillFix`). Taking a line off sends every line its list keeps,
each by its key, with `?replace=item` (or `expense`, or both), so NetSuite's
lines on that list become exactly those. Bills carry no sales tax.

Fix bill sends it once (`/api/purchasing/bill-fix`), as Fix invoice does.
The page sends back what it read the bill as (`billFacts`, as a short
signature): when NetSuite's bill has changed since, nothing is sent and the
page says to reload. The route works the fix out again from the bill as
NetSuite has it now, never from what the page worked out, and takes a claim
on the press (`bill_fixes`, `bill_fix_claim`, migration 472): pressing again
says the first press is being sent or went in, and only one fix of a bill
is sent at a time. Then it sends NetSuite the change (`patchVendorBill`). A
refusal, in NetSuite's own words (a closed month, say), is kept as refused:
nothing changed, and the same form can try again. When NetSuite doesn't
answer or faults, it's kept as unsure and the page says to reload and look.
A send that never finished holds the bill five minutes at most, then counts
as unsure. Each fix keeps who made it, why, what changed in words, what
NetSuite was sent, and NetSuite's total after, listed under Fixed before on
the screen and in the audit log (`bill_fixes`, `fix`). Nothing is emailed
to the vendor.

Books' own copy follows (`saveFixedOwnBill` in `src/lib/books/own-bills.ts`,
`gl_own_bill_fix`). When Books has its own copy of the bill (one FleetSuite
made: Parts Mail, CNI vendor invoices, installer payouts and Snap a bill),
the fix makes the copy's next version (`gl_own_bills.version`): its number,
date and lines as fixed, each part with the purchase order line it bills
(NetSuite's id for it, read with `getOrderLineIds`, five orders at most; a
line whose order can't be read goes without). The version before is kept
in `gl_own_bill_versions`, and a copy changed since the fix read it isn't
overwritten. Until the copy of NetSuite's bills (every two hours) has the
