bill as fixed, Own bills shows a copy that differs from it as waiting, and
the books post NetSuite's bill; then the check compares them again, its notes saying when
FleetSuite fixed the copy and how often (`gl_own_bill_pairs` gives each
copy's version and when it was last fixed), and the books post from the
copy again once its entry is NetSuite's. Saving it never stops a fix; one
that can't be saved is said on the screen in red, and the check shows the
copy differing until it's put right. A bill made in NetSuite has no copy of
FleetSuite's own, so only NetSuite's changes.

Credit all of it opens the vendor credit screen filled in to undo the bill
(`deepLinks.vendorCreditForBill`, GET `/api/purchasing/vendor-credit?bill=`):
its vendor, company and location, the number VOID- and the bill's number,
"Undoes bill B-100" as why, and a line for each account the bill posted
to, for what it posted there (`getBillPostings`, `creditAllOfBill` in
`src/lib/vendor-credit.ts`), so a part billed off a purchase order comes
back off the account received stock is held against, and no stock moves.
Once the vendor's open bills are read it's applied to the bill, for what's
open on it. The person checks it and makes it as any other vendor credit,
which needs vendor credits switched on. Its lines are entered by hand, and
the screen says why, when NetSuite hasn't posted the bill (it may be
waiting for approval), the bill takes money off an account (a discount),
what it posted doesn't come to its total, or it posted to more than 50
accounts.

Made-up example: bill B-100 from a parts vendor, nothing paid on it, has
shop supplies at $200.05, freight at $45.05, and two shelf units at $125.00
and a light at $50.00 billed off its purchase order: $545.10. The vendor's
invoice is B-100A, and its freight is $54.05. Fix: the number B-100 →
B-100A, the freight $45.05 → $54.05, why "Typed from the wrong invoice".
The screen shows the total going to $554.10, NetSuite's bill and Books'
copy both change, the parts stay billed off the purchase order, and the
copy keeps its first version.

### What isn't done yet

- Its vendor, company, location or terms can't be changed: credit it all
  and bill it again.
- After Credit all of it, the purchase order still counts the bill's parts
  billed: a vendor credit doesn't take them off the order.
- Before switching on, try fixes on a test bill of each kind, typed and
  billed off a purchase order, and check in NetSuite: that its answer names
  the total (`userTotal`) and each part's order and line (`orderDoc`,
  `orderLine`) as the screen reads them; that a line taken off keeps the
  other lines, each part still billed off its order (`?replace=`); that the
  order's billed quantities follow a part that changes or comes off; that a
  closed month refuses a fix; that NetSuite takes a vendor credit's line to
  the account received stock is held against (Credit all of it); and that a
  bill waiting for approval has posted nothing yet, so Credit all of it says
  so.

## Customer and vendor details changed in FleetSuite

From January 1, 2027 the team changes a customer's terms and credit limit,
and a vendor's email, phone, terms, addresses and 1099 details, in
FleetSuite, and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite (the NetSuite Exit
Checklist's January pieces, owner decisions 2026-10-31). A customer's name,
email, phone and address are changed on its page already (the Customer
Record's Edit panel, `updateCustomer`). It's built switched off
("Switches"): until a super admin turns it on, only super admins can open
the screens, to see what a change would send, and nothing goes to NetSuite.
Once it's on, anyone with Credit Applications who can see prices changes a
customer's terms, and More → Sales shows Customer Terms to them; anyone with
Payments who can see prices changes a vendor's details, and More → Money &
Books shows Vendor Details.

### A customer's terms

The screen is `/admin/customer-terms` (`deepLinks.customerTerms`). It finds
a customer in the copy of NetSuite's customer list by name, active ones
first, or opens on one: Change in the A/R card on a customer's page. It
reads the customer from NetSuite as it is now (`getEntityRecord`): its
terms, credit limit and what it owes (`parseTermsCustomer` in
`src/lib/customer-terms.ts`), with NetSuite's active terms from the copy of
its lists (`listTerms`). What a change can do (`planCustomerTerms`):

- **Its terms**, any of NetSuite's active ones. Its new invoices default to
  them, and FleetSuite's own invoices fall due by them; invoices already
  made keep theirs. Terms aren't taken off.
- **Its credit limit**, to the cent, $100,000,000 at most, or none: the box
  left blank sends NetSuite `creditLimit: null`.

### A vendor's details

The screen is `/admin/vendor-details` (`deepLinks.vendorDetails`). It finds
a vendor in the copy of NetSuite's vendor list by name, or opens on one. It
reads the vendor from NetSuite as it is now, its address book with it
(`getEntityRecord`, `expandSubResources`), in `parseDetailsVendor` in
`src/lib/vendor-details.ts`. What a change can do (`planVendorDetails`):

- **Its email and phone**: an email that looks like one, a phone of seven
  digits or more, or either left blank.
- **Its terms**, as a customer's: what its new bills default to, and what
  Snap a bill's bills fall due by.
- **Its addresses.** Any one changed, by NetSuite's id for its line in the
  vendor's address book, sent with everything NetSuite has on it (its third
  line, phone and country, as read) so nothing on it is lost. One added, a
  street and city at least, in the US: a vendor's first address is its
  billing and shipping address. And which one is its billing address
  (NetSuite's default billing address): the one marked before is unmarked.
  No address is taken off, and one NetSuite gave no id can't be changed.
- **Its 1099 details**: whether it gets a 1099 (`is1099Eligible`), its legal
  name as on its W-9, and its tax ID, nine digits, as 12-3456789 or
  123-45-6789. A vendor that gets a 1099 with no tax ID in NetSuite is noted
  on the screen: its 1099 needs one.

A tax ID is never kept in FleetSuite. NetSuite is sent the one typed; the
screen shows only the four digits NetSuite's ends with, what the page plans
against has only those, and the record of the change and the audit log say
"(ending 6789)" (`keptBody`). The database refuses a record of a change
that holds more of it (`detail_changes_no_tax_id`), NetSuite's words about
a change it refused are kept with the tax ID taken out (`withoutTaxId`),
and nothing sent to NetSuite is logged.

### Sending

Save to NetSuite sends it once (`/api/customer-terms`,
`/api/vendor-details`), as Fix bill does. The page sends back what it read
the record as (`customerFacts`, `vendorFacts`, as a short signature): when
NetSuite's record has changed since, nothing is sent and the page says to
reload. The route works the change out again from the record as NetSuite
has it now, never from what the page worked out, and takes a claim on the
press (`detail_changes`, `detail_change_claim`, migration 473): pressing
again says the first press is being sent or went in, and only one change of
a record is sent at a time. Then it sends NetSuite only what changed
(`patchEntityRecord`). A refusal, in NetSuite's own words, is kept as
refused: nothing changed, and the same form can try again. When NetSuite
doesn't answer or faults, it's kept as unsure and the page says to reload
and look. A send that never finished holds the record five minutes at most,
then counts as unsure. Each change keeps who made it, why, and what changed
in words, listed under Changed before on the screen and in the audit log
(`detail_changes`, `change_customer` and `change_vendor`). Nothing is
emailed to the customer or the vendor.

New terms go into the copy of NetSuite's customer or vendor list then too
(`noteTermsChanged`), not at its next read two hours on at most, since
Books' own invoices and Snap a bill read terms from it. One that can't be
noted is logged, and the next read catches up.

Made-up examples: a customer's credit application is approved at Net 30
with a $25,000.00 limit, and NetSuite has it on Due on receipt with no
limit. Change: terms Due on receipt → Net 30, credit limit no limit →
$25,000.00, why "Credit application approved". Its next invoice falls due
30 days on. A vendor sends a new W-9 with its legal name, Ridge Supply LLC,
its tax ID, 12-3456789, and a new remit address. Change: Gets a 1099 no →
yes, Legal name (none) → Ridge Supply LLC, Tax ID (none) → one ending 6789,
New address: PO Box 100, Springfield, MO 65801, and that address marked as
its billing address. The record of the change says "(ending 6789)".

### What isn't done yet

- A new customer or vendor is made where it is now: a vendor with Create in
  NetSuite on the vendor pickers (`/api/cni/create-vendor`), its terms and
  1099 details then set here.
- No address is taken off, and a vendor's name, its company and its
  currency aren't changed here.
- Before switching on, try changes on a test customer and a test vendor and
  check in NetSuite: that `creditLimit: null` takes a limit off; that a
  vendor's GET with `expandSubResources` gives `addressBook.items` with an
  id on each line; that an address changed by its line's id keeps the
  vendor's other addresses (the address book is merged, not replaced) and a
  line with no id is added; that marking a billing address unmarks the one
  before; that NetSuite takes `taxIdNum` written 12-3456789 and its GET
  gives it back, whole or masked, so the screen can show its last four
  digits; and that `is1099Eligible` and `legalName` are what the vendor
  record calls them.

## Item accounts changed in FleetSuite

In the first quarter of 2027 FleetSuite becomes the master for items, with
each item's income, cost and inventory accounts (the NetSuite Exit
Checklist's "Customers, vendors and items"; owner decisions 2026-10-31).
Item Accounts changes the accounts NetSuite posts an item's documents to,
and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite. It's built switched off
("Switches"): until a super admin turns it on, only super admins can open
the screen, to see what a change would send, and nothing goes to NetSuite.
Once it's on, anyone with Payments who can see prices changes an item's
accounts, and More → Money & Books shows Item Accounts to them.

The screen is `/admin/item-accounts` (`deepLinks.itemAccounts`). It finds an
item in the copy of NetSuite's items by number or display name, active ones
first, or opens on one: Change accounts beside each item set up wrong on
Stock by item, and on its own page. It reads the item from NetSuite as it is
now (`getItemRecord`). NetSuite files each kind of item under a record type
of its own and the copy says only roughly which (a service, a non-inventory
item), so the record types that kind can be are read in turn until one has
it: a service for sale, for resale, then for purchase. What a change can do
(`planItemAccounts` in `src/lib/item-accounts.ts`), by the accounts its kind
of item has:

| Account | What posts to it | It can be | Which items have it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Income account | Its sales | An income or other income account | Every kind but those only bought |
| Cost of goods sold account | What it cost, when it ships | A cost of goods sold account | Inventory items and assemblies |
| Inventory asset account | What it cost, while it's on the shelf | A current asset account | Inventory items and assemblies |
| Expense account | Its bills | A cost of goods sold, expense or other expense account | Non-inventory items, services and other charges bought for resale or only bought |

Each changes only to an account of BMG's that takes postings now (not
inactive or a summary account), from the chart of accounts, and only to one
of the kind it can be: an inventory asset account is never a cost of goods
sold account, which is how two items came to have theirs swapped ("Items set
up wrong, and value left over"). An account can't be taken off. An account
of the wrong kind on the item now is marked in red, and a change that leaves
one so says it. A kit, and a non-inventory item, a service or an other
charge for sale, has an income account only. Discounts, payments,
descriptions, subtotals and item groups aren't changed here.

The screen shows the cost of goods sold account, which the copy of
NetSuite's items can't read (NetSuite answers 500 for it, "NetSuite's
lists"), since it reads the item's own record.

### Sending

Save to NetSuite works as on Vendor Details: it sends once
(`/api/item-accounts`); when the item changed in NetSuite since the page read
it, nothing is sent and the page says to reload; the route works the change
out again from the item as NetSuite has it now, and takes a claim on the
press (`detail_changes`, record type `item`, migration 478), one change of an
item at a time. NetSuite is sent only the accounts that changed
(`patchItemRecordFields`), and its refusal, in its own words, is kept as
refused; when it doesn't answer or faults, the change is kept as unsure and
the page says to reload and look. Each change keeps who made it, why, and
what changed in words, listed under Changed before and in the audit log
(`change_item_accounts`).

The copy of NetSuite's items takes the new income, inventory asset and
expense accounts then too (`noteItemAccountsChanged`), not at its next read
two hours on at most, since the posting rules read an item's accounts from
it and Stock by item lists the items whose asset account isn't an asset
account by it: an item put right leaves that list at once.

Documents already made keep what they posted; the item's next ones post to
the new accounts. What an inventory item is worth on the shelf stays on its
old inventory asset account until a journal entry moves it (Journal
Entries), and the screen says so when the inventory asset account changes.

Made-up example: 51-2002, a stocked shelf kit, has 58000 Cost of Goods Sold
as its inventory asset account and 19000 Inventory Asset as its cost of
goods sold account. Change: Cost of goods sold account 19000 Inventory Asset
→ 58000 Cost of Goods Sold, Inventory asset account 58000 Cost of Goods Sold
→ 19000 Inventory Asset, why "Set up with its accounts swapped". It leaves
Stock by item's list of items set up wrong at once.

### What isn't done yet

- A new item still gets the accounts it's made with in NetSuite (an
  inventory item 19000, 58000 and 47900, `inventoryItemAccounts` in
  `src/lib/netsuite.ts`; others NetSuite's defaults); they're changed here
  afterwards.
- An item's accounts are BMG's: an item in another company's subsidiary
  isn't offered that company's accounts.
- Before switching on, try a change on a test item of each kind and check
  in NetSuite: that a GET for a service, a non-inventory item or an other
  charge as the wrong one of sale, resale or purchase answers 404, so the
  next is tried; that `incomeAccount`, `cogsAccount`, `assetAccount` and
  `expenseAccount` are what each kind's record calls them, a kit's income
  account included; that the item's record answers with its cost of goods
  sold account, which SuiteQL answers 500 for; and that NetSuite takes a
  changed inventory asset account on an item with stock on hand.

## Estimates in FleetSuite only

In January 2027 estimates stop going to NetSuite (the NetSuite Exit
Checklist's "Estimates in FleetSuite only"; owner decisions 2026-10-31).
It's built switched off ("Switches"), the other way round from the other
pieces: while it's off, estimates push to NetSuite as they do now, and
once a super admin turns it on, nobody pushes or syncs one
(`src/lib/estimates-in-fleetsuite.ts`, migration 479).

Once it's on:

- The estimate's Push to NetSuite as Estimate button goes, and a grey line
  says estimates stay in FleetSuite and NetSuite gets the sales order. The
  page asks `GET /api/estimates/push` whether it's on.
- `POST /api/estimates/push` refuses a first push and a sync alike (409,
  `estimatesStayInFleetsuite: true`) before reading anything, so a page
  opened before the switch can't push either.
- `POST /api/wrap-quote/netsuite`, the wrap quote's old NetSuite quote,
  refuses too. The wrap quote page already makes a FleetSuite estimate
  first (`/api/wrap-quote/open-estimate`) and doesn't call it.
- FleetSuite AI is told not to send anyone to push an estimate.

What stays: Convert to Sales Order makes the sales order in NetSuite from
FleetSuite's own lines, as it always has, and it never needed a NetSuite
estimate. An estimate pushed before keeps its NetSuite copy and its
"Pushed to NetSuite" banner and View PDF; its banner says NetSuite's copy
closes with the sales order, because converting it still closes that
copy (`closeNetSuiteEstimate`), and deleting the estimate still deletes it
in NetSuite first. Every NetSuite estimate is still copied into FleetSuite
every two hours (`src/lib/netsuite-estimate-sync.ts`), so one made in
NetSuite still shows in search.

Made-up example: on January 4, 2027 Sam opens EST-2701-004 for a new
upfit. The estimate has no Push button, so Sam sends it to the customer
from FleetSuite, the customer approves, and Convert to Sales Order makes
SO2201 in NetSuite with the estimate's lines. NetSuite never sees an
estimate for the job.

### What isn't done yet

- An estimate pushed before the switch and changed after it keeps the
  NetSuite copy as it was pushed, until the sales order closes it.

## FleetSuite's record wins

From the first quarter of 2027 FleetSuite is the master for customers,
vendors and items (owner decision 2026-10-09, which ends the 2026-09-21 rule
that NetSuite's record is the master), and from January 1, 2027 the team
changes them only in FleetSuite (the NetSuite Exit Checklist's "FleetSuite
the master for customers, vendors and items"). Until now the NetSuite syncs
copy a change made in NetSuite over FleetSuite's record. Part 48 stops that
for customers and vendors (`src/lib/fleetsuite-records.ts`, migration 480),
and part 49 for items, on the same switch. It's built switched off
("Switches") and, like "Estimates in FleetSuite only", the other way round:
while it's off, the syncs work as they do now and nothing is listed.

Once it's on:

- The customer syncs, every two hours (`/api/cron/netsuite-sync`) and Sync
  from NetSuite (`/api/netsuite/customers`), keep the name, email, phone and
  billing address FleetSuite has for a customer it has, in the CRM record
  (`prospects`) and the customer list (`customers`) alike. NetSuite's fills
  one FleetSuite has blank. Sync from NetSuite doesn't read addresses, so it
  leaves a known customer's address to the two-hourly sync.
- A contact FleetSuite has for a customer is left as it is, and a new one
  made in NetSuite still comes in (the two-hourly sync, Sync from NetSuite
  and `/api/netsuite/contacts/sync`, which also notes NetSuite's ID on a
  contact FleetSuite has by the same name without one).
- The vendor list copy (`src/lib/vendor-master.ts`) keeps the name, email
  and phone FleetSuite has for a vendor in the same way. Vendor Details
  (`/api/vendor-details`) writes the email, phone and terms it sent to
  NetSuite into FleetSuite's copy too, so the next copy doesn't list them.
- The parts syncs, every hour (`/api/cron/parts-sync`,
  `syncPartsIncremental` in `src/lib/parts-sync.ts`) and Sync from NetSuite
  on the Parts page (`/api/parts/sync`), keep the item number, display
  name, description, purchase price, vendor and labor hours FleetSuite has
  for a part (`netsuite_parts`). NetSuite's fills one FleetSuite has blank,
  and one both have blank falls back as before: a display name to the
  number, a description to the display name. A display name that's only
  the part's number, a description that's only its display name or number,
  and a purchase price of 0 count as blank, since that's how the syncs wrote
  a part NetSuite had none for; labor hours of 0 are a value, no labor
  charged (migration 258). A generic part's labor isn't looked at
  (migration 462), and when Sync from NetSuite couldn't read NetSuite's
  costs, FleetSuite's purchase price stays as it is, unlooked at. The sales
  price was already FleetSuite's (`resolveSalesPrice`).
- Looking a part up by its number (`findOrMirrorPart`, the scans' "is this
  part real?") finds the part FleetSuite has for that NetSuite item under
  its own number, bringing it back if it was put away, instead of writing
  NetSuite's number and names over it.
- A customer, vendor or item FleetSuite hasn't got comes in as now, and what
  only NetSuite keeps still comes: a customer's spend, entity ID and parent,
  a vendor's terms and whether it's inactive, and an item's type, class,
  department, stock and whether it's taxable or inactive.

Where NetSuite has something else for one of those fields, the sync lists
it (`record_differences`, written by `note_record_differences`): one row for
each field of a record, open until it's dealt with. A sync that sees it
again updates the open row with what each has now, and one that finds the
two the same again closes it by itself. A name is compared as written, an
email in any case, a phone by its digits with or without the leading 1, an
address's lines in any case, and extra spaces never count. An item's number
and vendor are compared in any case, its display name and description as
written, its purchase price to the cent and its labor hours to the
hundredth. Where NetSuite has none for an item, FleetSuite's stays and
nothing is listed: NetSuite's display names, vendors and labor hours are
mostly blank, and listing each would bury the rest. A value longer than
1,000 characters, as only an item's description gets, is listed cut short
(`NOTED_TEXT`), so it can't fail the sync that notes it, and NetSuite's
can't be taken from the list then. A sync that
can't tell whether the switch is on (`recordsWinForSync`), or can't read
FleetSuite's records, stops rather than write over what it couldn't see: a
switch that can't be read counts as off everywhere else, and here off would
copy NetSuite's over FleetSuite's with nothing listed. One that can't note
what it found says so, and the two-hourly sync and the hourly parts sync,
which then don't move on, and the vendor copy see it again next time;
Sync from NetSuite on the Parts page says so in its answer.

Both parts syncs now write their parts in groups that name the same
columns (`upsertPartRows`). One write of parts naming different columns
writes every column any of them names, and a part without one got nothing
there: a part NetSuite has no labor hours for lost FleetSuite's whenever
another part in the same 200 had NetSuite's. That was so before part 49
too, switched off or on.

Books → Changed in NetSuite (`/admin/books/changed-in-netsuite`,
`deepLinks.booksChangedInNetsuite`) shows the open ones, newest first, each
with what FleetSuite and NetSuite have and a link to the record in
NetSuite, and the last 25 dealt with. A super admin taps:

- **Keep FleetSuite's:** FleetSuite sends its value to NetSuite, a
  customer's as the Customer Record's Edit panel does (`updateCustomer`), a
  vendor's by changing its vendor record (`patchEntityRecord`), and an
  item's number, display name, description or purchase price through the
  item script (`updateItemFields`, `scripts/netsuite-item-restlet.js`) as
  the Parts page's edits do, which must say it wrote the field: one that
  answers without it is an older script that needs replacing, and nothing
  changed. An item's vendor and labor hours stay in FleetSuite (NetSuite
  keeps an item's vendor as a link to a vendor record, and the script
  doesn't write labor), so keeping FleetSuite's sends nothing and it's
  listed again only if NetSuite's changes. When NetSuite refuses, nothing
  changed and it stays open. When FleetSuite can't tell whether NetSuite
  took it, it stays open too, and the next sync closes it once NetSuite has
  FleetSuite's. One that FleetSuite has meanwhile made the same as
  NetSuite's closes without sending anything, and one where FleetSuite has
  none now can only be taken.
- **Take NetSuite's:** FleetSuite's record takes NetSuite's value, a
  customer's in the CRM record and the customer list, an item's in its
  part. An item number another part in FleetSuite already has, in any case,
  can't be taken. Nothing goes to NetSuite.

Each asks first, is claimed before it's done so a second tap does nothing,
and is audited (`POST /api/admin/books/changed-in-netsuite`,
`keep_fleetsuite_record`, `take_netsuite_record`). While the switch is off,
nothing on the list can be kept or taken. The daily check tells the super
admins about each new one once ("Daily check").

Made-up example: on January 6, 2027 someone changes Acme Fleet Services'
email in NetSuite to ap@acmefleet.example. FleetSuite has
billing@acmefleet.example, so the next sync keeps it and lists the email.
The next morning Craig opens the notification and taps Keep FleetSuite's,
and FleetSuite sends billing@acmefleet.example to NetSuite. On January 8
someone changes part C4-RA24-3's purchase price in NetSuite to $14.25 and
its labor hours to 2. FleetSuite has $12.50 and 1.5 hours, so the next
hourly parts sync keeps both and lists them. Keep FleetSuite's on the
purchase price sends $12.50 to NetSuite through the item script; on the
labor hours it keeps 1.5 in FleetSuite and sends nothing.

### What isn't done yet

- Keeping FleetSuite's item number, display name, description or purchase
  price needs the item script in NetSuite to be the current
  `scripts/netsuite-item-restlet.js`, as the Parts page's edits do.

## Payments received in FleetSuite

Payments, deposits and banking stay in NetSuite on January 1, 2027 and move
into FleetSuite during 2027, each before its move, with FleetSuite sending
each on to NetSuite so nobody types anything twice (owner decisions
2026-10-31). Part 54 is the first piece: a customer's check or ACH payment
received in FleetSuite and put on that customer's open invoices
(`src/lib/receive-payment.ts`, migration 485). Card payments wait on
eBizCharge; deposits on estimates and orders are part 55 ("Deposits taken in
FleetSuite"), and bank deposits part 56 ("Bank deposits made in
FleetSuite").
It's built switched off ("Switches") and stays off past January 1: until a
super admin turns it on, only super admins can open the screen, to see what
it would send, and nothing goes to NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with
Invoicing who can see prices can.

On Invoicing → Invoiced each open NetSuite invoice has a Payment button,
which opens `/invoices/payment?invoice=<NetSuite id>`
(`deepLinks.receivePayment`). The screen reads the invoice, its customer and
that customer's open invoices as NetSuite has them now, oldest first, with
what's still open on each (`getPaymentInvoices`: status Open and
`foreignamountunpaid`, as Financials and the agings read them). Only the
invoice's own company's invoices are listed, since a payment pays one
company's; any others are counted in a line. It also lists the payments
FleetSuite received from the customer in the last 60 days, made or being
made. Then the person fills in (`workOutPayment`):

- **Check or ACH**, with the check's number, which a check must have, or the
  ACH payment's reference. NetSuite's memo reads "Check 10442" or
  "ACH 0187", then the memo typed.
- **The day it was received**, not after today nor more than a year back.
- **The amount and what it pays on each invoice.** The screen starts with
  the invoice Payment was pressed on, paid in full; Oldest first spreads the
  amount over the open invoices oldest first, as NetSuite's own Accept
  Payment does. Every dollar received goes on an invoice, and none pays
  more than NetSuite has open on it now.

Receive payment sends it once. The page makes an id when it opens, the
press's, and the route (`/api/invoices/payment`) works the payment out again
from what NetSuite has open now, never from what the page sent, and sends
nothing that doesn't fit. A check whose number FleetSuite already has from
that customer, as a payment or a deposit, made or being made, is turned
away; a second check with the same number takes a letter after it. Then the
route takes a claim on the
press (`received_payments`, `received_payment_claim`), looks in NetSuite for
a payment under the press's External ID (`fleetsuite-cp-<id>`,
`findCustomerPayment`), and only when there's none makes it
(`createCustomerPayment`: a customer payment in Undeposited Funds, applied
to exactly the invoices picked with the amount on each, NetSuite's own
auto-apply off). NetSuite never takes two payments under one External ID
either.

- **Made**: FleetSuite keeps NetSuite's id and number on the press, reads
  the invoices back and says in red when what NetSuite shows open on one
  isn't what it had less what the payment paid (`appliedAsSent`), keeps
  Books' own copy, and audits it. Pressed again, it says what the first
  press made.
- **Refused**: the claim is let go with NetSuite's reason in red
  (`received_payment_let_go`), and the same form can be sent again.
- **Not sure** (no answer, a fault on NetSuite's side, or no id in its
  answer): it's held. Ask NetSuite, from five minutes on, looks for it by
  its External ID: found, it's made; not there, it's let go and nothing was
  paid. The send is in the NetSuite feed too, which keeps unsure ones on
  top until someone has looked ("NetSuite feed").

Nothing is emailed to the customer.

Books keeps its own copy of each one as it was received and sent, rather
than read back from NetSuite (`gl_own_payments` and
`gl_own_payment_applications`, saved only by `gl_own_payment_save`,
`src/lib/books/own-payments.ts`): the company, the day, check or ACH with
its number or reference, NetSuite's memo, the amount, that it went to
Undeposited Funds, and what it paid on each invoice. Saving it never stops
a payment; one that can't be saved is said on the screen in red.

Made-up example: Acme Plumbing owes $400.10 on INV1990 and $250.00 on
INV2001, and its check 10442 for $650.10 comes in on October 30. Payment on
INV2001 starts with $250.00 on INV2001; typing $650.10 and pressing Oldest
first puts $400.10 on INV1990 and $250.00 on INV2001. Receive payment makes
one NetSuite payment of $650.10 in Undeposited Funds, its memo
"Check 10442", and both invoices are paid.

### Before switching on

- FleetSuite's NetSuite role needs to make customer payments and to see
  them, and each invoice's open amount, in SuiteQL. Until it can see a
  customer payment, FleetSuite can't tell one that isn't there from one it
  can't see, and sends nothing.
- The field names it sends (`undepFunds`, `autoApply`, `checkNum` and the
  `apply` list) are NetSuite's REST names, not yet tried on this account:
  check the first real payment in NetSuite, the invoices it paid, and that
  it's in Undeposited Funds.

### What isn't done yet

- Card payments (eBizCharge). Bank deposits, which move the money from
  Undeposited Funds to the bank, are part 56 ("Bank deposits made in
  FleetSuite").
- A payment that pays more than the customer's open invoices, takes a
  discount, or applies a credit memo or deposit along with the money.
- A list of payments received beyond the screen's 60 days. Books' own
  copies aren't checked against NetSuite's payments or posted yet; until
  they are, NetSuite's payment posts from the copy of NetSuite's ledger as
  any payment does ("Payments").

## Deposits taken in FleetSuite

After payments on invoices (part 54) come deposits on estimates and orders:
a customer's check or ACH deposit taken in FleetSuite on a sales order and
sent on to NetSuite (part 55, `src/lib/take-deposit.ts`, migration 486).
Card deposits wait on eBizCharge. It's built switched off ("Switches") and
stays off past January 1: until a super admin turns it on, only super admins
can open the screen, to see what it would send, and nothing goes to
NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with Invoicing who can see prices can.

A deposit goes on a sales order, because that's what NetSuite keeps it
against: a sales order's page has a Deposit button, and so does an estimate
once it has its sales order, by its Open sales order button. Both open
`/sales-orders/deposit?so=<NetSuite id>` (`deepLinks.takeDeposit`), shown
only to those who can use it (`/api/sales-orders/deposit?status=1`); the
sales order's is hidden once the order is billed, closed or cancelled. The
screen reads the order as NetSuite has it now (`getDepositSalesOrder`: its
customer, company, status and total), and the deposits on it already: the
ones taken in FleetSuite, made or being made, and the ones the books' copy
of NetSuite's deposits links to the order, which is copied every two hours
("Copying deposits"), each once. Then the person fills in
(`workOutDeposit`):

- **Check or ACH**, with the check's number, which a check must have, or the
  ACH payment's reference. NetSuite's memo reads "Check 10442" or
  "ACH 0187", then the memo typed.
- **The day it was received**, not after today nor more than a year back.
- **The amount**, no more than what's left of the order after the deposits
  on it.

It goes only on an order NetSuite still has open: pending approval,
fulfillment or billing (statuses A, B, D, E and F). A billed order takes the
payment on its invoice instead ("Payments received in FleetSuite"); a
closed or cancelled one takes nothing.

Take deposit sends it once, the way Receive payment does. The page makes an
id when it opens, the press's, and the route (`/api/sales-orders/deposit`)
works the deposit out again from the order as NetSuite has it now and the
deposits on it, never from what the page sent, and sends nothing that
doesn't fit. A check whose number FleetSuite already has from that customer,
as a deposit or a payment, made or being made, is turned away; a second
check with the same number takes a letter after it. Then the route takes a
claim on the press (`received_deposits`, `received_deposit_claim`), looks in
NetSuite for a deposit under the press's External ID (`fleetsuite-cd-<id>`,
`findCustomerDeposit`), and only when there's none makes it
(`createCustomerDeposit`: a customer deposit on the order, in Undeposited
Funds as payments go now).

- **Made**: FleetSuite keeps NetSuite's id and number on the press, keeps
  Books' own copy, and audits it. Pressed again, it says what the first
  press made.
- **Refused**: the claim is let go with NetSuite's reason in red
  (`received_deposit_let_go`), and the same form can be sent again.
- **Not sure** (no answer, a fault on NetSuite's side, or no id in its
  answer): it's held. Ask NetSuite, from five minutes on, looks for it by
  its External ID: found, it's made; not there, it's let go and none was
  taken. The send is in the NetSuite feed too ("NetSuite feed").

NetSuite puts an order's deposits on the order's invoice when the order's
billed, as it does now; FleetSuite doesn't apply them itself. Nothing is
emailed to the customer.

Books keeps its own copy of each one as it was taken and sent, rather than
read back from NetSuite (`gl_own_deposits`, saved only by
`gl_own_deposit_save`, `src/lib/books/own-deposits.ts`): the company whose
order it's on, the order, the day, check or ACH with its number or
reference, NetSuite's memo, the amount, and that it went to Undeposited
Funds. Saving it never stops a deposit; one that can't be saved is said on
the screen in red.

Made-up example: Acme Plumbing's SO1450 comes to $12,500.40, and its check
10442 for $2,500.50, half down, comes in on October 30. Deposit on SO1450
shows $12,500.40 left; Take deposit makes one NetSuite deposit of $2,500.50
on SO1450 in Undeposited Funds, its memo "Check 10442 · half down". The
next deposit on SO1450 has $9,999.90 left for it.

### Before switching on

- FleetSuite's NetSuite role needs to make customer deposits and to see
  them, and each sales order's status and total, in SuiteQL. Until it can
  see a customer deposit, FleetSuite can't tell one that isn't there from
  one it can't see, and sends nothing.
- The field names it sends (`salesOrder`, `payment`, `undepFunds` and
  `checkNum`) are NetSuite's REST names, not yet tried on this account:
  check the first real deposit in NetSuite, that it's on its order and in
  Undeposited Funds, and that NetSuite puts it on the order's invoice when
  the order's billed.
- A deposit made in NetSuite counts against its order only once the books'
  copy links it to the order, which isn't yet seen in NetSuite's data
  ("Checking deposits"): check that a deposit made in NetSuite shows on the
  screen under "Deposits on this order".

### What isn't done yet

- Card deposits (eBizCharge), refunding a deposit, and applying one to an
  invoice by hand.
- A deposit on an estimate that has no sales order yet: it waits for the
  order.
- Books' own copies aren't checked against NetSuite's deposits or posted
  yet; until they are, NetSuite's deposit posts from the copy of NetSuite's
  deposits as any deposit does ("Customer deposits").

## Bank deposits made in FleetSuite

After payments on invoices (part 54) and deposits on orders (part 55), both
made in NetSuite in Undeposited Funds, comes the bank deposit that moves
them to the bank (part 56, `src/lib/bank-deposit.ts`, migration 487). It's
built switched off ("Switches") and stays off past January 1: until a super
admin turns it on, only super admins can open the screen, to see what it
would send, and nothing goes to NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with
Invoicing who can see prices can.

Invoicing → Invoiced has a Bank deposit button, shown only to those who can
use it (`/api/invoices/bank-deposit?status=1`), which opens
`/invoices/bank-deposit` (`deepLinks.bankDeposit`). The screen lists what
NetSuite has waiting in Undeposited Funds for a company now, as NetSuite's
own Make Deposits does: the customer payments and customer deposits NetSuite
shows as not deposited, from the last year, oldest first
(`getUndepositedFunds`), each with its number, day, customer, check number
or reference, and amount. One another press is depositing now is marked
and can't be ticked. Then the person picks (`workOutBankDeposit`):

- **The company**, BMG or 1084, each with what it has waiting.
- **The bank account it went into**, one of the company's bank accounts in
  Books' chart from NetSuite: active, not a heading, and limited to the
  company's subsidiary in NetSuite (or, where NetSuite didn't say, under
  the company in Books).
- **The day it went to the bank**, not after today, more than a year back,
  or before the payments and deposits in it.
- **The payments and deposits that went together**, ticked, up to 100,
  with Tick all; the total shows as they're ticked.
- **A memo**, if any.

Make deposit sends it once, the way Receive payment does. The page makes an
id when it opens, the press's, and the route (`/api/invoices/bank-deposit`)
works the bank deposit out again from what NetSuite has waiting now, never
from what the page sent, and sends nothing that doesn't fit: a payment that
isn't waiting any more, or ticked ones whose total isn't what the page
showed, is turned away to reload. Then the route takes a claim on the press
(`made_bank_deposits`, `made_bank_deposit_claim`), which holds the payments
and deposits in it: while it's being made, another press with any of them
is turned away, so none goes into two bank deposits. It looks in NetSuite
for a bank deposit under the press's External ID (`fleetsuite-bd-<id>`,
`findBankDeposit`), and only when there's none makes it
(`createBankDeposit`: one NetSuite Deposit into the account, on the day,
with the payments and deposits ticked, each marked deposited).

- **Made**: FleetSuite keeps NetSuite's id and number on the press, reads
  NetSuite's total back, keeps Books' own copy, and audits it. When
  NetSuite's total isn't what FleetSuite sent, Books keeps no copy and says
  in red to look at it in NetSuite. Pressed again, it says what the first
  press made.
- **Refused**: the claim is let go with NetSuite's reason in red
  (`made_bank_deposit_let_go`), its payments are free again, and the same
  form can be sent again.
- **Not sure** (no answer, a fault on NetSuite's side, or no id in its
  answer): it's held, and so are its payments. Ask NetSuite, from five
  minutes on, looks for it by its External ID: found, it's made; not there,
  it's let go and none was made. The send is in the NetSuite feed too, under
  Bank deposits ("NetSuite feed").

No alerts go out, and nothing is emailed to anyone.

Books keeps its own copy of each one as it was sent, rather than read back
from NetSuite (`gl_own_bank_deposits`, saved only by
`gl_own_bank_deposit_save`, `src/lib/books/own-bank-deposits.ts`): the
company, the bank account, the day, the memo, the payments and deposits in
it with each one's amount, and the total. Saving it never stops a bank
deposit; one that can't be saved is said on the screen in red. NetSuite's
bank deposit comes back through the copy of NetSuite's general ledger like
any other and posts from it ("Bank deposits, transfers and refunds").

Made-up example: Rivera Plumbing's payment PYMT-9001 of $1,250.00 and Ozark
Fleet Co's deposit DEP-77 of $2,500.00 are waiting in Undeposited Funds, and
both go to the bank on October 30 on one slip. Ticking both shows
$3,750.00; Make deposit into 10100 Operating Checking makes one NetSuite
bank deposit of $3,750.00 dated October 30, and neither is waiting any more.

### Before switching on

- FleetSuite's NetSuite role needs to make bank deposits, and to see
  customer payments, customer deposits and bank deposits in SuiteQL. Until
  it can see them, FleetSuite can't tell what's waiting, or a bank deposit
  that isn't there from one it can't see, and sends nothing.
- What's waiting is read from each payment's and deposit's status as
  NetSuite names it ("Not Deposited", `BUILTIN.DF(t.status)`), and a
  check's number from `otherrefnum`; neither is tried on this account yet:
  check that the screen lists what NetSuite's Make Deposits does for the
  same company.
- The field names it sends (`payment.items`, each with `id` and `deposit`)
  are NetSuite's REST names, not yet tried on this account: check the first
  real bank deposit in NetSuite, that it's in the account picked with the
  payments ticked, and that they're no longer in Undeposited Funds.
- A customer deposit NetSuite has already put on its order's invoice may
  show as applied rather than not deposited, and so not be listed: check
  one that's waiting.

### What isn't done yet

- Cash back, other deposits (money that isn't a customer's payment or
  deposit), and card payments (eBizCharge).
- A list of the bank deposits made in FleetSuite. Books' own copies aren't
  checked against NetSuite's bank deposits or posted yet; until they are,
  NetSuite's bank deposit posts from the copy of NetSuite's ledger as any
  bank deposit does ("Bank deposits, transfers and refunds").
- Matching bank deposits to the bank's own lines, which waits on the
  banking choices.

## Vendors paid in FleetSuite

After the money coming in (parts 54 to 56) comes the money going out: open
bills paid in a payment run, asked for approval, approved by a second
person, and made in NetSuite as bill payments (part 57,
`src/lib/pay-vendors.ts`, migration 488). It's built switched off
("Switches") and stays off past January 1, until paying vendors moves into
FleetSuite during 2027: until a super admin turns it on, only super admins
can open the screen, to make and save runs and see what they'd pay, and none
is asked for approval or sent to NetSuite. Once it's on, anyone with
Payments who can see prices can.

More → Money & Books → Pay Vendors, shown only to those who can use it
(`/api/pay-vendors?status=1`), opens `/admin/pay-vendors`
(`deepLinks.payVendors`). It lists the company's payment runs: waiting for
approval, being paid, drafts, and the last ones paid. + New payment run
reads the bills NetSuite has open for the company now (`getOpenBills`:
NetSuite's Open status and its own open amount on each), vendor by vendor,
oldest due first, each with its number, due day and what's open, and the
person picks (`workOutRun`):

- **The company**, BMG or 1084.
- **The bank account it pays from**, one of the company's bank accounts:
  the same list a bank deposit picks from (`src/lib/books/bank-accounts.ts`).
- **The day it's paid**, not more than a month ahead or a year back.
- **The bills**, ticked under each vendor, with Tick all. A ticked bill
  pays what's open on it, or a smaller amount typed to pay part of it. Each
  vendor with a bill ticked gets one payment in the run, by check or ACH.
  Up to 50 vendors, 100 bills a vendor and 500 bills a run.
- **A memo**, if any, on each bill payment in NetSuite; an ACH payment's
  starts "ACH".

A bill another run holds (one asked for approval, or approved with a payment
of that bill not made or dropped yet) is marked "in another run" and can't
be ticked. Save keeps the run as a draft (`vendor_payment_runs`,
`vendor_payment_run_save`). Anyone who can use the screen can open a draft,
change it and save it again; whoever started it, or a super admin, can
delete it.

**Asking for approval.** Ask for approval checks the run against NetSuite
again (`getBillsNow`): each bill still open, still that vendor's, with at
least what the run pays on it still open, and none held by another run. A
run that doesn't fit is turned away with every reason, in red.
`vendor_payment_run_ask` asks for the draft as the asker saw it (one saved
again since is turned away to reload) and holds its bills, and an alert goes
to the super admins ("A payment run is waiting for approval", with the
company, day, bank account, vendors, bills and total). Whoever started it or
asked for it, or a super admin, can take it back to change it.

**Approving.** A super admin who didn't ask approves it, or sends it back
with why, which whoever asked and whoever started it read on the run and in
an alert. Approve and pay checks it against NetSuite once more and approves
the ask the approver saw (`vendor_payment_run_approve`), which makes one
payment for each vendor from the run as it was asked
(`vendor_payment_run_payments`), and an alert tells whoever asked and whoever
started it. Then the
page makes them in NetSuite one at a time, each its own press.

**Making each payment.** The route takes the claim on the payment
(`vendor_payment_claim`), checks its bills against NetSuite just before it
sends, looks in NetSuite for a bill payment under its External ID
(`fleetsuite-vp-<id>`, `findVendorPayment`), and only when there's none
makes it (`createVendorPayment`: NetSuite's Pay Bills for one vendor, from
the bank account picked, on the day, applied to exactly its bills, each with
what it pays; a check is made To Be Printed).

- **Made**: FleetSuite keeps NetSuite's id and number on the payment, reads
  NetSuite's total back, keeps Books' own copy, and audits it. When
  NetSuite's total isn't what FleetSuite sent, Books keeps no copy and says
  in red to look at it in NetSuite. The run is paid once each of its
  payments is made or dropped.
- **Refused**: NetSuite's reason shows in red on the payment
  (`vendor_payment_let_go`). Make it again sends it again, or a super admin
  drops it from the run with why, and its bills stay open in NetSuite
  (`vendor_payment_drop`).
- **Not sure** (no answer, a fault on NetSuite's side, or no id in its
  answer): it's held, and the page stops making the rest. Ask NetSuite, from
  five minutes on, looks for it by its External ID: found, it's made; not
  there, it's refused and none was made. The send is in the NetSuite feed
  too, under Bill payments ("NetSuite feed").

Its checks print on BMG's check stock from the run ("Checks printed on
BMG's stock"), and its ACH payments are paid from a file made for First
Bank from the run ("The ACH file for First Bank"). The alerts also go to
anyone with "Copy me on new alerts" ticked, never to whoever pressed;
nothing is sent to a vendor.

Books keeps its own copy of each bill payment as it was sent
(`gl_own_bill_payments`, saved only by `gl_own_bill_payment_save`,
`src/lib/books/own-bill-payments.ts`): the company, the vendor, the bank
account, the day, check or ACH, the memo, each bill it paid and how much,
the total, and who asked and approved. Saving it never stops a payment; one
that can't be saved is said on the screen in red. NetSuite's bill payment
comes back through the copy of NetSuite's general ledger like any other and
posts from it.

Made-up example: Ozark Hose & Fitting has two open bills, B-4410 for $812.40
due October 20 and B-4471 for $1,100.00 due November 5, and Gateway Steel
has GS-209 for $2,450.00. A BMG run from 10100 Operating Checking dated
November 6 ticks all three, with $600.00 typed on B-4471 to pay part of it,
Ozark by check and Gateway by ACH: two bill payments, $1,412.40 and
$2,450.00, $3,862.40 in all. Once a second super admin approves it, NetSuite
has a check To Be Printed to Ozark and an ACH payment to Gateway, and B-4471
has $500.00 still open.

### Before switching on

- FleetSuite's NetSuite role needs to make bill payments, and to see bills
  and bill payments in SuiteQL. Until it can see bill payments, FleetSuite
  can't tell one that isn't there from one it can't see, and sends nothing.
- The open bills are read from each bill's status, A (Open), and
  `foreignamountunpaid`; a bill waiting for approval in NetSuite isn't open
  and isn't listed. Neither is tried on this account yet: check that the
  screen lists what NetSuite's Pay Bills does for the same company.
- The field names it sends (`account`, `toBePrinted`, and `apply.items`,
  each with `doc`, `apply` and `amount`) are NetSuite's REST names, not yet
  tried on this account: check the first real bill payment in NetSuite, that
  it's from the account picked and pays the bills ticked by what was typed,
  and that a check shows in Print Checks.

### What isn't done yet

- Vendor credits and early-payment discounts taken in a run, and bills in a
  currency other than dollars.
- A list of the bill payments made in FleetSuite. Books' own copies aren't
  checked against NetSuite's bill payments or posted yet.

## Checks printed on BMG's stock

A payment run's checks print on BMG's pre-printed check stock from the run
itself, instead of NetSuite's Print Checks (part 58,
`src/lib/check-printing.ts`, `src/lib/check-pdf.ts`,
`src/components/PayVendorChecks.tsx`, `/api/pay-vendors/checks`, migration
489). It rides on paying vendors' switch (`vendor_payments`): until that's
on, super admins can line up the stock and print a test page, and no check
is numbered or sent to NetSuite. Once it's on, whoever can pay vendors can.

**What's printed.** One check a letter-size sheet: the check in one part of
the sheet and a stub in each of the other two, the vendor's and BMG's. The
stock carries the bank's details, the MICR line, the check number and the
signature line already, so FleetSuite prints only the date, the payee, the
amount in figures (stars before it) and in words (stars after it, to the
end of the line), the vendor's address for a window envelope, and the memo.
Each stub has the vendor, the check number, the day and memo, the bank
account, each bill it pays with its date and what's paid on it (twelve
lines at most, the rest as one line), and the total. The payee is NetSuite's
Print on Check As for the vendor, or its name when that's blank. Checks are
signed by hand.

**Lining up the stock.** Pay Vendors → Check stock → Line up the check
stock, or Line up the stock on a run's Checks, sets a bank account's checks
(`vendor_check_layouts`): where the check is on the sheet (top, middle or
bottom), and the print moved right or left and down or up, a sixteenth of
an inch a press, an inch at most. Print a test page prints a made-up check
the way it's set, saved or not, marked TEST · NOT A CHECK · VOID, with the
stock's perforations drawn. Print it on plain paper at actual size, hold it
against a sheet of the stock up to the light, and move the print until each
line sits where it should, then Save. A bank account never set prints at the
top, not moved. Each save is audited.

**Numbering.** An approved run's Checks lists each vendor it pays by check:
to print once its bill payment is made, and then the check it has. Tick the
ones to print (all start ticked) and type the number on the first check in
the printer, suggested as one past the highest check number used on the
bank account in FleetSuite's checks or NetSuite's copy of the ledger
(`vendor_check_last_number`). Number gives the ticked checks that number and
the ones after it, in the run's order:

1. NetSuite's bill payments are read first (`getChecksNow`). Each has to be
   there, not voided, the vendor's and for what FleetSuite made it for, and
   still To Be Printed; one that isn't is left out with why, in red. One
   printed from NetSuite's Print Checks already keeps NetSuite's number and
   shows as printed from NetSuite. When NetSuite can't be read, nothing is
   numbered.
2. Each number has to be unused on the bank account: in FleetSuite's checks,
   spoiled ones too, in NetSuite's copy of the ledger (its checks, bill
   payments and refunds on that account, `vendor_check_numbers_used`), and
   in NetSuite itself (`getCheckNumbersUsed`). One that's used stops it,
   with which, and nothing is numbered.
3. The numbers are claimed (`vendor_checks_claim`: each number once on a
   bank account, one check a bill payment), each with what's printed on it
   (`printed_as`), so printing it again prints the same thing.
4. Each number is set in NetSuite in turn (`setCheckNumber`: the bill
   payment's Check # set and To Be Printed taken off, so NetSuite doesn't
   print it too). It stops at the first NetSuite refuses or doesn't answer,
   so the checks to print run on from the first number with none missed,
   and lets go of the ones after it, to number next. One NetSuite didn't
   answer about is held: Ask NetSuite, from five minutes on, finds whether
   NetSuite took it (numbered) or not (let go). Once it's taken, the bill
   payment's number in FleetSuite and in Books' own copy is the check's. A
   long run stops in time to answer, and the rest number with the next
   press.

A vendor NetSuite has no address for gets a warning: its check shows only
the name in the envelope's window.

**Printing.** The numbered checks show as to print, in number order, with
where the stock goes ("Put check 1050 on top of the stock in the printer").
Print opens them as a PDF, a check a sheet, laid out the bank account's way:
print at actual size, not fit to page. Then say how they came out: All
printed right (or Printed right on each), or Didn't print right on one that
jammed or printed crooked, with why. That one is kept with its number and
why (write VOID across it), and its vendor's check is ready to number again
from the next check in the printer; FleetSuite knows NetSuite's bill payment
still has the spoiled number and replaces it. Use it only while the check is
still in hand: one that went out is voided in NetSuite instead.

Numbering, printing, didn't print right and letting a number go are audited
on the run (`number_checks`, `print_checks`, `spoil_check`, `let_go_check`),
with the check numbers and no amounts. Nothing alerts.

Made-up example: the November 6 run above makes Ozark's check To Be Printed
in NetSuite. Jessie puts the stock in the printer with check 1050 on top,
types 1050 and presses Number 1 check, and NetSuite's bill payment becomes
check 1050, no longer To Be Printed. Print shows check 1050 to Ozark Hose &
Fitting for **1,412.40, "One thousand four hundred twelve and 40/100", with
B-4410 $812.40 and B-4471 $600.00 on both stubs. It jams: Didn't print
right, "Jammed". Number 1 check from 1051 makes the bill payment check 1051,
and 1050 is kept as spoiled.

### Before switching on

- Setting a bill payment's Check # and To Be Printed (`tranId`,
  `toBePrinted`) through NetSuite's REST API isn't tried on this account
  yet, nor reading `tobeprinted`, `voided` and the vendor's
  `printoncheckas` in SuiteQL, nor finding check numbers by bank account
  (`transactionaccountingline.account`): number one check and check it in
  NetSuite.
- Print a test page on plain paper against a sheet of BMG's stock, for each
  bank account that writes checks, and save where it lines up.

### What isn't done yet

- A positive pay file for the bank, and voiding a check in FleetSuite.
- Checks written outside a payment run, which still print from NetSuite's
  Print Checks.

## Vendor bank numbers kept encrypted

A vendor's bank numbers for ACH, kept in FleetSuite so it can make the ACH
file NetSuite makes today (part 59, `src/lib/vendor-bank.ts`,
`src/lib/vendor-bank-lock.ts`, `/api/pay-vendors/bank`,
`src/components/VendorBankNumbers.tsx`, migration 490). Owner decision
2026-10-09: FleetSuite keeps vendor bank numbers encrypted, shows them
masked, and has a second person confirm a change on a call-back. That ends,
for vendors paid by ACH, the 2026-06-29 rule that FleetSuite never keeps
bank numbers (`docs/cni-redesign.md` §2.4); a vendor's tax ID is still never
kept. The ACH file for First Bank (part 60) pays to them, and it rides on
paying vendors' switch.

Super admins only, switched on or off: Pay Vendors → Vendor bank numbers →
Open bank numbers (`deepLinks.vendorBank`, which an alert's link opens at
the vendor).

**Entering them.** + Bank numbers for a vendor, find the vendor in
FleetSuite's copy of NetSuite's vendor list, and type from the vendor's ACH
form: the routing number, the account number twice, checking or savings,
the name on the account (22 characters at most, as the ACH file has it,
started from the vendor's name), and where the numbers came from (whose
form, and how it reached BMG). The routing number has to be one: 9 digits,
starting 01 to 12, 21 to 32 or 61 to 72, with its check digit right. The
account number is 4 to 17 digits, spaces and dashes left out. Save for a
call-back keeps them waiting for a call-back, and every other super admin,
and Craig, gets an alert (`books_vendor_bank_waiting`). A vendor has one
set waiting at most; new numbers for a vendor with confirmed ones replace
those once they're confirmed.

**Confirming them on a call-back.** A super admin who didn't enter them
calls the vendor on a number BMG already had, not one from the form or the
email that sent it. The page shows the vendor's phone on file and, in red,
when it was changed in FleetSuite (Vendor Details) in the last 90 days, so
the call goes to a number from before that. Confirm on a call-back, type the
phone called and who read the numbers out, and type the routing number,
account number and checking or savings as the vendor reads them: they're
never read to the vendor. When it matches what was entered, they're
confirmed, sealed by the server, and replace the vendor's confirmed numbers
before. When it doesn't, nothing changes and it says which didn't match;
the third call-back that doesn't match refuses them. Confirming and a
refusal after three tell the other super admins and Craig
(`books_vendor_bank_decided`).

**Refusing and removing.** Refuse on numbers waiting, or Remove on
confirmed ones, with why. Nothing is paid to numbers refused, and nothing
is paid to a vendor by ACH once theirs are removed, until new ones are
entered and confirmed. Both tell the other super admins and Craig. Past
ones lists the last 50 replaced, refused or removed, with why.

**What's kept.** `vendor_bank_accounts` keeps, to show, the routing number,
the four digits the account number ends with, checking or savings and the
name on the account. The numbers themselves are kept only locked
(`account_locked`, AES-256-GCM with a key from VENDOR_BANK_KEY, locked to
the vendor and the set they were entered as), and what's locked is what's
paid to. Only a set waiting or confirmed keeps its locked numbers. The page
never gets more than the last four digits back, and no audit, alert or log
has more than that. No note holds a run of six digits or the account
number, so a number never sits in words. Entering, each call-back that
didn't match, confirming, refusing and removing are audited
(`vendor_bank_accounts`), with the vendor, routing number, last four digits
and checking or savings.

What it guards against:

- **A copy of the database** has only the locked numbers, which don't open
  without the key, and the key is only in Vercel.
- **The database changed by hand.** Locked numbers moved to another vendor
  or set don't open; a set made confirmed without the server's seal, or
  shown with a routing number, last four digits or checking or savings
  other than what's locked, shows in red, and one waiting can't be
  confirmed.
- **One person.** Whoever enters them never confirms them, in the page,
  the route and the table.
- **An email asking BMG to pay new numbers.** The call-back goes to a phone
  BMG already had, a phone changed lately is called out, and the vendor
  reads the numbers, not BMG.
- **Guessing.** Three call-backs that don't match refuse them.

**The key.** VENDOR_BANK_KEY is 32 random bytes as base64. Until it's set,
none can be entered, and the card says so with Make a key, which makes one
in the browser and sends it nowhere. Add it in Vercel → Settings →
Environment Variables as VENDOR_BANK_KEY for Production, redeploy, and keep
a copy in a password manager: without it the numbers can't be opened, and
every vendor's are entered and confirmed again. Changing the key does the
same; numbers locked with another key say so in red. System Health lists it
with the other keys.

Made-up example: Dana at Ozark Hose & Fitting emails an ACH form on October
30. A super admin enters routing number 123456780, a checking account
ending 9012, "OZARK HOSE & FITTING", from "ACH form emailed by Dana at
Ozark, 10/30", and the other super admins get the alert. One of them calls
Ozark on 636-555-0142, the number on file from before, and Dana reads out
the routing number and the account number. They match what was entered,
and Ozark's numbers are confirmed: the page shows Checking •••• 9012.

### What isn't done yet

- Bringing vendors' numbers over from NetSuite's Electronic Payments: each
  is entered from the vendor's form and confirmed on a call-back. The
  privacy statement says the Service doesn't keep bank account and routing
  numbers from NetSuite or QuickBooks, which holds while none are brought
  over.
- Nothing sends them to NetSuite, and NetSuite's own copy isn't changed.

## The ACH file for First Bank

A payment run's ACH payments are paid by First Bank from a file FleetSuite
makes from the run, the NACHA file NetSuite's Electronic Payments makes
today (part 60, `src/lib/ach-file.ts`, `/api/pay-vendors/ach`,
`src/components/PayVendorAch.tsx`, migration 491). It rides on paying
vendors' switch (`vendor_payments`): until that's on, super admins can set
up how First Bank takes the file, and no file is made or downloaded. It's
for super admins only, switched on or off, because the file holds whole
account numbers.

**How First Bank takes the file.** Pay Vendors → ACH file for First Bank →
Set up the ACH file, or How First Bank takes it on a run's ACH file, sets
each bank account's file (`vendor_ach_setups`), as First Bank set BMG up to
send it:

- First Bank's routing number, where the file goes, and the bank's name in
  the file.
- BMG's ACH ID with First Bank (10 characters, or 9 digits) and BMG's name
  for First Bank.
- The sending bank: the first 8 digits of First Bank's routing number,
  filled in from it.
- The company name (16 characters at most) and BMG's company ID for ACH
  (often 1 and the EIN).
- The description vendors see on their statement (10 characters at most,
  VENDOR PAY to start).
- Businesses (CCD) or people (PPD).
- Whether each payment lists the bills it pays, for the vendor.
- Whether the file has a debit to BMG's account that offsets the payments
  (a balanced file), and how its lines end.

Read NetSuite's last ACH file, picked or pasted, fills all of it from the
last file NetSuite made for that bank account. The file is read in the
browser, and only how it starts leaves it: its payments are only counted.
Each save is audited, with routing numbers and no account number.

**A balanced file.** When First Bank wants the offsetting debit in the file,
BMG's account the payments come from is typed from BMG's own records,
twice, with checking or savings. It's never taken from NetSuite's file. The
privacy statement says the Service doesn't keep bank account and routing
numbers from NetSuite or QuickBooks, and that holds: what's kept from
NetSuite's file is how it starts, whose only routing number is First Bank's
own, the bank's public number. Reading NetSuite's file says the last four
digits of the account it debits, so the right one is typed. It's kept locked
like a vendor's numbers (`offset_locked`, locked to that bank account), and
the page shows only its last four digits.

**Making the file.** An approved run with ACH payments shows ACH file for
First Bank to super admins: each ACH payment, the account it pays to (the
routing number and last four digits), and why one isn't ready (its bill payment isn't made
in NetSuite yet, no numbers are confirmed on a call-back for the vendor, or
those kept don't open as they're locked). The ready ones start ticked. Make
the ACH file asks first, then:

1. Each payment has to be one of this run's ACH payments, made in NetSuite,
   and in no other file, unless that one was set aside.
2. Each vendor's numbers confirmed on a call-back now are opened as they're
   locked; one that doesn't open, or shows anything other than what's
   locked, stops it, with why.
3. The file is one batch: a credit to each vendor's account for what its
   bill payment paid, with NetSuite's number for the bill payment as the
   reference the vendor sees and, when set, an addenda listing the bills it
   pays (`RMR*IV*B-4410**812.40\`, as many as fit in 80 characters). A
   balanced file ends the batch with the debit to BMG's account for the
   total.
4. First Bank pays it on the run's day, or the first banking day after it
   when that's a weekend or a Federal Reserve holiday, and never sooner than
   the next banking day, by BMG's clock (Central time).
5. Each file made for a day to First Bank gets a letter telling it apart, A
   to Z then 0 to 9: 36 a day at most.
6. It's kept locked to its own id (`file_locked`), with each payment in it
   (`vendor_ach_file_payments`), and a payment in a file can't go in
   another. Pressed twice, it's one file.

An alert tells the other super admins and Craig (`books_ach_file_made`):
the file's name, the run, how many vendors it pays and the day First Bank
pays it, with no amounts.

**Downloading and uploading.** Download the file downloads it as it was
made (`ACH-BMG-2026-11-04-A.txt`), counted on the file. It's downloaded only
while it waits to be uploaded, and not once a vendor's numbers in it were
replaced or removed after it was made. Upload it in First Bank's online
banking, then Uploaded to First Bank marks it sent: its locked copy goes, so
it can't be downloaded again. A file not uploaded by its day to be paid
says so in red: set it aside and make a new one.

**Setting aside.** Set aside, with why, is for a file that won't be
uploaded, or one First Bank refused or didn't pay: its locked copy goes, and
its payments can go in a new file. On a file marked uploaded it
warns first that a file First Bank paid would pay them twice. An alert tells
the other super admins and Craig (`books_ach_file_set_aside`).

**What's kept.** For each file (`vendor_ach_files`): its name, when it was
made and its letter, the day First Bank pays it, how many payments, the
total, the entry hash First Bank checks it with, who made, downloaded,
uploaded or set it aside and when, and why it was set aside (never with a
run of six digits, so no account number sits in words). For each
payment in it: the vendor, the routing number, the last four digits,
checking or savings, the name on the account and what it pays. The whole
account numbers are only in the locked file while it waits to be uploaded,
and in the vendors' locked numbers. Making, downloading, uploading and
setting aside are audited, with routing numbers and last four digits at
most. Nothing is sent to NetSuite: the bill payments are made there already
("Vendors paid in FleetSuite").

Made-up example: the November 6 run above pays Gateway Steel $2,450.00 by
ACH. Once its bill payment is made in NetSuite as 1052, and Gateway's
numbers are confirmed on a call-back (savings ending 1234), a super admin
makes the file on Wednesday, November 4 at 9:15: `ACH-BMG-2026-11-04-A.txt`,
one credit to Gateway's savings account for $2,450.00, reference 1052, with
`RMR*IV*GS-209**2450.00\` for Gateway, paid Friday, November 6. They
download it, upload it in First Bank's online banking, and mark it
uploaded.

### Before switching on

- Ask First Bank for its ACH file specifications, and check each bank
  account's setup against them: its routing number and name, BMG's ACH ID
  and company ID, CCD, whether it wants a balanced file, and how lines end.
  Reading NetSuite's last file for the account fills them in.
- Have First Bank check a test file, or make a first one paying one vendor
  a small amount, before a whole run.
- FleetSuite dates each file the next banking day at the soonest: check
  that's in time for First Bank's cut-off.

### What isn't done yet

- Prenotes, the zero-dollar entry that tries a vendor's new numbers before
  the first payment.
- Returns and notices of change from First Bank, which are read in its
  online banking: a returned payment is voided in NetSuite and paid again.
- Sending the file to First Bank from FleetSuite: it's downloaded and
  uploaded by hand.

## 1084's rent and mortgage

1084 Cool Springs LLC owns the building BMG rents, and its only business is
BMG's rent coming in and the mortgage going out (owner, 2026-10-10, thread
"Replacing NetSuite with FleetSuite"). Books → Rent and mortgage
(`/admin/books/rent-and-mortgage`, `deepLinks.booksRentMortgage`) shows its
months from January 2025 through this month, newest first, and makes the
two entries that book each one (part 61, `src/lib/books/rent-and-mortgage.ts`,
`/api/admin/books/rent-and-mortgage`, migration 492). It's the first slice
of the NetSuite Exit Checklist's "1084's monthly entries"; 1084's bank
reconciliation is the second ("1084's bank reconciliation").

**The setup** (`rent_mortgage_setups`) picks each account by NetSuite's id:
1084's bank account, its rent income, BMG's rent (the account BMG's books
keep its rent on), 1084's mortgage, the mortgage's interest, and escrow when
the lender holds some. Each is one its company can use for a typed line, of
a kind it can be, and picked once. The lender's name goes in the payment's
memo. A month's checks wait on the accounts they read.

**Each month** (`rent_mortgage_months`) is the rent received from BMG and
its day, and the lender's statement for the payment made in the month: the
day it was paid, what went to principal, interest and escrow, and the
principal left after it. Each is dated in the month the books show it in,
so a payment made on March 5 is March's. Both are typed: no lease amount is
kept, and the rent starts at what BMG's books have for the month, else last
month's. A save says so when someone else saved the month since the page
was opened.

**The checks.**

- The rent: what BMG's books have on its rent account in the month, debits
  less credits, next to what 1084's have in rent income, credits less
  debits. Green when they're the same; grey while BMG's have it and 1084's
  don't yet, in this month or while the month's rent entry is on its way;
  red otherwise, with how far apart they are.
- The mortgage: what 1084's books have paid off the mortgage and in
  interest in the month, and the principal they leave at its end (NetSuite's
  balance before 2025 and every month's moves since), next to the
  statement's. Green when all three are the statement's; red naming each
  that isn't; grey while no statement is typed, or the books have nothing
  paid in the month yet. Escrow isn't held against the books: what the
  lender pays out of it, the taxes and the insurance, moves its account too.
  The bank reconciliation catches the payment's total.
- A grey note says when a statement's principal left doesn't follow from
  last month's: what last month's left, less this month's principal.

**The month's entries.** Make the month's entries makes two typed journal
entries ("Journal entries typed in FleetSuite") as drafts in the general
journal, for 1084, worked out from the figures as saved, never the page's:

- the rent received, dated the day it came in: 1084's bank debited and its
  rent income credited, memo "Rent from BMG Fleet Installations for March
  2027";
- the mortgage payment, dated the day it was paid: principal, interest and
  escrow each debited, a $0 one left out, and the bank credited with what
  they come to, memo "Mortgage payment to (the lender) for March 2027".

Each is dated its own day because each is its own move in 1084's bank, so
the bank reconciliation can tick each. They're asked for approval, approved
by a super admin and sent on to NetSuite on Journal entries like any other,
so they ride the journal entries switch: until it's on, only super admins
make them, to see what they'd make, and none is asked for approval or sent.
Making them needs Payments too, as the general journal does. Made again, a
draft is brought up to date with the figures and the setup, and the page
says when one is behind. What an entry books stays as it is once it's asked
for approval: changing the rent or the statement it books is refused until
the entry is taken back. Deleting a draft on Journal entries lets go of it,
and the month's next one is made new; a figure taken off the month leaves
its draft for someone to delete.

Whoever opens the books sees the page. The bookkeeper and super admins type
the setup and the figures.

### What isn't done yet

- Reading the lender's statement from its PDF: it's typed.
- What 1084 pays besides the mortgage, such as insurance or taxes paid
  outside escrow: that's typed on Journal entries.

## 1084's bank reconciliation

Books → 1084's bank reconciliation (`/admin/books/bank-reconciliation`,
`deepLinks.booksBankReconciliation`) holds 1084 Cool Springs' bank
statements against its books, month by month: each entry on its bank
account ticked as it clears the bank, until the statement's starting
balance and what cleared come to its ending balance (part 62,
`src/lib/books/bank-reconciliation.ts`, `/api/admin/books/bank-reconciliation`,
migration 493). It's the second slice of the NetSuite Exit Checklist's
"1084's monthly entries", after "1084's rent and mortgage". The bank
account is the one Rent and mortgage's setup picks; until one is picked,
the page says so.

**The entries** are the documents on the account as Reports' detail names
them (`gl_books_account_documents`, migrations 400 and 460), each with a day
and an amount in FleetSuite's books, at what FleetSuite's books have for it,
money in positive. One that NetSuite's copy has and FleetSuite's books
haven't posted yet is counted in a grey note and shows once it's posted. An
account with more than 20,000 entries isn't reconciled here, and the page
says so in red.

**Where reconciling starts** (`bank_rec_starts`) is typed once: the first
month whose statement is reconciled here, the bank's balance on the last
day of the statement before it (a day in the month before; December 31,
2024 for January 2025, as FleetSuite's books start the day after), and each
entry the books had by then that hadn't cleared the bank yet. Every other
entry dated by then counts as cleared already. The page holds it against
the books: their balance on that day, less what hadn't cleared, next to the
bank's, green when they tie and red with how far apart they are. A start
that doesn't tie is still saved, since that's worth knowing rather than a
reason to refuse it. It changes only while no month is done, and moving it
to another month lets go of what's saved on the months, which the page
warns of first.

**Each month** (`bank_recs`, `bank_rec_ticks`) is the statement's last day
(in the month; its last day until it's changed), its ending balance, and
each entry ticked as it cleared on it, listed as Money in and Money out.
A month lists each entry the books have by the statement's last day that
didn't clear on a month before; of those dated by where reconciling starts,
only the ones picked as not cleared then. The page shows the starting
balance (the month before's ending balance, or where reconciling starts),
what's ticked in and out, the cleared balance, and the difference from the
ending balance, as each is ticked. Finish reconciling makes the month done
once the difference is $0.00, which the database checks again to the cent.
What each entry comes to is what the books have for it when it's saved,
never the page's. Months are reconciled in order, through this one: only
the month after the last one done is ticked, and an entry clears once.

A month done keeps what cleared at what each came to then. When the books
change one since, its amount or gone from them, the month says so in red.
Each month also says whether the books tie to the bank: their balance on the
statement's last day, less what hadn't cleared by then, next to the ending
balance.

**Undo** opens the last month done again, with what was ticked on it, for
a reason of 300 characters at most, kept with who and when
(`bank_rec_events`). It's refused while the month after it has entries
ticked: untick them there first.

A save says when someone else saved since the page was opened. Whoever
opens the books sees the page; the bookkeeper and super admins reconcile.
Nothing here moves money or is sent to NetSuite, so it has no switch. The
audit records carry no amounts: the month, the statement's last day, how
many entries were ticked, and why a month was undone.

### What isn't done yet

- Reading the bank's statement from its PDF or a download: the ending
  balance is typed and each entry ticked.
- BMG's own bank accounts: the tables are by company and bank account, for
  the banking piece to use, but the page is 1084's only.
- A bank fee or interest the books don't have yet: it's typed on Journal
  entries, then ticked here.

## Adjustments approved before they're posted

Inventory Adjust (`src/lib/inventory-adjust.ts`, migration 454) posts an
admin's adjustment of a part's stock at O'Fallon to NetSuite at once, as an
Inventory Adjustment to 52000 Inventory Adjustment. Once approving
adjustments is switched on (`adjustment_approvals`, "Switches"), each one
waits for approval first, and a super admin who didn't ask approves it
before it goes to NetSuite (part 63, the first slice of the NetSuite Exit
Checklist's "Inventory moves and value", migration 494,
`src/lib/inventory-adjust-approvals.ts`). The plan adds it during 2027,
from April, so it stays off on January 1. Switched off, Adjust works as it
always has.

**Asking.** Switched on, the Adjust pop-up (an Inventory page row, or a
part's details on the Parts list) says Ask for approval instead of Post to
NetSuite. The entry is checked as Adjust checks it, and the change it makes
is worked out then against NetSuite's live count at O'Fallon: the number
added or taken away, or the count on the shelf less NetSuite's count. That
