change is what's posted once it's approved, so a shelf count stays what the
count found even if stock moves before it's approved. A count that makes a
change that isn't a whole number (NetSuite showing 9.5) can't be asked for.
It's kept in `inventory_adjustment_asks` (`inventory_adjustment_ask`) under
an id the pop-up makes, so a request sent twice is kept once. One adjustment
waits per part and location at a time: while one is open, the pop-up shows
it, and nothing else is asked for or posted for that part, switched on or
off. A switch or a list of asks that can't be read stops Adjust, rather
than post one unapproved.

**Deciding.** Adjustments to approve (`/admin/inventory/approvals`,
`deepLinks.adjustmentsToApprove`, linked from the Inventory page while it's
switched on or any are open) lists the ones not decided yet, oldest first,
and the 30 decided last. Admins open it, as Adjust. A super admin who
didn't ask:

- approves it: FleetSuite posts it as Adjust does, as the change worked out
  when it was asked (`adjustInventory`, mode `delta`), and NetSuite's memo
  and Adjust's history say "by" whoever asked, "approved by" the super
  admin. Approving takes the claim first (`inventory_adjustment_claim`), so
  a second press finds it being posted. NetSuite's refusal, or Adjust's own
  (stock moved and it would go below 0), puts it back to wait with why in
  red (`inventory_adjustment_let_go`). When FleetSuite lost touch with
  NetSuite, or NetSuite answered with something other than a refusal (the
  NetSuite feed's rule), NetSuite may have posted it, so it's kept as not
  known (`inventory_adjustment_unsure`);
- or turns it down, saying why (300 characters at most), which whoever
  asked sees on the page and in the pop-up (`inventory_adjustment_turn_down`).

Whoever asked can withdraw it while it waits (`inventory_adjustment_withdraw`).
One NetSuite didn't say about, or a press still posting it after five
minutes (longer than a press takes), is settled by a super admin who looks
in NetSuite: It's in NetSuite, with NetSuite's number if they have it, or
It isn't in NetSuite, and it waits to be approved again
(`inventory_adjustment_settle`). Ones asked while it was on can be approved
or turned down after it's switched off too. Nobody is alerted, as Adjust
alerts nobody (Craig, 2026-10-30). The audit records name the part, the
change and the reason, with no amounts.

Made-up example: Jessie counts 12 LR-48 ladder racks on the shelf while
NetSuite shows 9, so she asks for +3 (Count correction). Craig approves it,
and NetSuite gets an adjustment of +3 with the memo "FleetSuite: Count
correction · by Jessie, approved by Craig", even though NetSuite's count
had moved to 10 by then.

### What isn't done yet

- A threshold: once it's on, every adjustment waits, however small.
- An adjustment settled as in NetSuite by hand isn't in Adjust's history,
  since FleetSuite never heard NetSuite's id for it, so Books posts it from
  NetSuite's copy, as it does an adjustment made in NetSuite.
- Before switching on, agree who approves, since only super admins can.

## Inventory value at a month's end

`/admin/books/inventory-value` (Books → Inventory value, part 64, the
second slice of the NetSuite Exit Checklist's "Inventory moves and value")
shows what each stocked item was worth at the end of a month, item by item
at each location, and how that comes to each inventory account on the
balance sheet that day, every cent between them named, so a month can be
closed with stock that ties. It's what NetSuite's Inventory Valuation
report gives as of a period's end, worked out from FleetSuite's own copies.
Read only: nothing is written to NetSuite or to the books, and there is no
switch.

**The read.** Migration 495's `gl_stock_month_end(p_month, p_limit)` reads
the same copies as Stock by item, cut off at the end of the month asked for
(from January 2025). Each document counts in the month NetSuite posted it
to, as the copy of its general ledger has it, else in its date's month, so
an item's count and its value are cut off alike and the value ties to the
ledger's balance that day: an item receipt dated February 28 that NetSuite
posted to March is in March on both. An item's count at a month's end is
its count on December 31, 2024 plus every line that moved it through the
month; its value, for an item NetSuite keeps in stock, is its starting
value plus what NetSuite posted to an inventory account on its lines
through the month. A stock document gone from NetSuite counts for nothing
on its items; while the copy of NetSuite's ledger still has it, it shows
among the documents with no stocked item until the next copy drops it.

**Tying it to the balance sheet.** For each company and inventory account
(`accountTie` in `src/lib/books/inventory-value.ts`), the page starts from
the stocked items on it at the end of the month before, adds what moved
them in the month by kind of document, and comes to where they stood at
its end. From there to FleetSuite's balance sheet, each piece is a line of
its own:

1. where NetSuite's ledger and the items stood apart on December 31, 2024,
   before FleetSuite's copies begin;
2. the documents since that posted to the account on no stocked item's
   stock line, a journal entry say, the month's listed first and then the
   biggest, up to 100, each opening its check on Invoices vs NetSuite when
   FleetSuite posts its kind;
3. anything else on the copy of NetSuite's ledger, in red as not accounted
   for: none, unless the copy is off;
4. what NetSuite's own totals hold that the copy of its ledger doesn't yet,
   which the next copy catches up;
5. how far FleetSuite's books are from NetSuite's own totals, in red.

So the stocked items plus those five come to FleetSuite's balance sheet,
and each total along the way (NetSuite's ledger as FleetSuite copies it,
NetSuite's balance sheet, FleetSuite's) is the one Reports shows: the
starting entry is the same on every side, as on Reports. A fixed asset
account a stocked item names is tied the same way; most of what's on one is
the company's own equipment or vehicles, so the page says so.

**The page.** Last month at first, any month from January 2025 through this
one, with a button per company when there's more than one. It warns when
the month isn't over yet, when the stock copy never ran, failed or stopped
for time, when the last posting run failed or stopped for time, and when
NetSuite's own totals aren't read as far as the month. The items list each
item at each location with how many were on hand, what one cost on average
and what they were worth, marking fewer than none on hand in red and value
left with none on hand in amber, each item opening its moves on Stock by
item. Each account links to its documents on Reports from January 2025
through the month.

**Excel.** Download Excel (`/api/admin/books/inventory-value/download`,
`inventoryWorkbook` in `src/lib/books/report-workbook.ts`) gives the
company and month shown as a file named like "BMG Inventory value
2026-10-31.xlsx": the items, the tie to each account a line at a time, and
the documents with no stocked item, with what the page warns about at the
top.

```ts
const { data } = await service.rpc('gl_stock_month_end', { p_month: '2026-10-01', p_limit: 100 });
const value = monthEndValue(data); // value.companies[0].accounts[0].apart === start + entries.total + unexplained + notCopied + booksApart
```

Made-up example: at the end of March, BMG's ladder racks and bins on 1200
Inventory come to $2,339.00. NetSuite's ledger stood $100.00 below the
items on December 31, 2024, and a February journal wrote $30.00 of shrink
off the account, so the copy of NetSuite's ledger holds $2,209.00.
NetSuite's own totals hold $20.00 more that the copy hasn't caught up on,
$2,229.00, and FleetSuite's books, $20.00 short of NetSuite's, hold
$2,209.00: $130.00 under the items, every cent of it on a line.

### What isn't done yet

- A count of each item at a month's end against a count on the shelf: the
  Count screen in 2027 ends in one approved adjustment.
- Each item's value is what NetSuite posted on its lines. Once stock
  documents are made in FleetSuite alone, their value has to come from
  FleetSuite's own cost instead.
- It hasn't been put next to NetSuite's own Inventory Valuation report for
  a month yet, which is worth doing before closing the first month in
  FleetSuite's books.

## Switches

Each piece that takes the team out of NetSuite is built switched off, one
row each in `books_switches` (migrations 466 to 473, 478 to 480, 485 to
488 and 494, `src/lib/books/switches.ts`), and stays off until a super admin turns it on
in Books → Switches (`/admin/books/switches`, `deepLinks.booksSwitches`),
which whoever opens the books can read. The plan turns them on on January 1,
2027, asking the owner once in late December, all but `customer_payments`,
`customer_deposits`, `bank_deposits` and `vendor_payments`, which stay off
until receiving payments, taking deposits, bank deposits and paying vendors
move into FleetSuite during 2027, and `adjustment_approvals`, which the plan
adds during 2027, from April; their cards on the page say so (`later`).

While a piece is off, the people who'd use it don't see it at all; super
admins can open its screen and see what it would send, and its route
refuses to send anything to NetSuite, for everyone. A switch that can't be
read counts as off. Every change says who made it and when, on the page and
in the audit log (`switch_on`, `switch_off`). `estimates_in_fleetsuite` and
`fleetsuite_records` are the other way round: on, the first stops estimates
going to NetSuite and the second stops NetSuite's changes coming over
FleetSuite's records, so off leaves things as now, and the page asks with
their own words (`turnOn`, `turnOff`). A sync that can't read
`fleetsuite_records` stops instead of counting it as off (`readSwitch`).
`adjustment_approvals` is too: on, an adjustment made with Adjust waits for
approval before it goes to NetSuite, so off leaves Adjust as now, and Adjust
stops when it can't read it rather than post one unapproved.

| Piece | What it does once it's on | Where |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `credit_memos` | Anyone with Invoicing makes a credit memo against an invoice in FleetSuite, and FleetSuite makes it in NetSuite ("Credit memos made in FleetSuite") | Invoicing → Invoiced → Credit on an invoice |
| `vendor_credits` | Admins with Parts Ordering enter a vendor's credit in FleetSuite and apply it to that vendor's open bills, and FleetSuite makes it in NetSuite ("Vendor credits made in FleetSuite") | More → Purchasing → Vendor Credits |
| `card_charges` | Anyone with Payments adds the Chase card's activity and codes each charge to its account, and FleetSuite sends each month's charges to NetSuite as one journal entry ("Card charges coded in FleetSuite") | More → Money & Books → Card Charges |
| `journal_entries` | Anyone with Payments types a journal entry, and the CPA its own adjustments, and asks for approval, a super admin who didn't ask approves it, and FleetSuite makes it in NetSuite ("Journal entries typed in FleetSuite") | More → Money & Books → Journal Entries; the CPA's, Books → CPA adjustments |
| `inventory_transfers` | Admins move stock from one of a company's locations to another in FleetSuite, and FleetSuite makes the transfer in NetSuite ("Inventory transfers made in FleetSuite") | More → Parts & Inventory → Inventory Transfers |
| `invoice_fixes` | Anyone with Invoicing fixes an invoice after it's made, its PO number, memo and VIN, and while nothing on it is paid its date and lines, and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite and in its own copy ("Invoices fixed in FleetSuite") | Invoicing → Invoiced → Fix on an invoice |
| `bill_fixes` | Admins with Parts Ordering fix a bill after it's made, its number, memo and due date, and while nothing on it is paid its date and lines, and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite and in its own copy ("Bills fixed in FleetSuite") | More → Purchasing → Fix a Bill, or Fix on Books → Own bills |
| `customer_vendor_details` | Anyone with Credit Applications changes a customer's terms and credit limit, and anyone with Payments a vendor's email, phone, terms, addresses and 1099 details, and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite ("Customer and vendor details changed in FleetSuite") | More → Sales → Customer Terms, or Change on a customer; More → Money & Books → Vendor Details |
| `item_accounts` | Anyone with Payments changes an item's income account, an inventory item's cost of goods sold and inventory asset accounts, and the expense account of one that's bought, and FleetSuite makes the change in NetSuite ("Item accounts changed in FleetSuite") | More → Money & Books → Item Accounts, or Change accounts on Books → Stock by item |
| `estimates_in_fleetsuite` | Estimates stay in FleetSuite: nobody pushes or syncs one to NetSuite, and NetSuite gets each job's sales order when it's made ("Estimates in FleetSuite only") | An estimate's buttons, where Push to NetSuite as Estimate goes |
| `fleetsuite_records` | A change made in NetSuite to a customer's name, email, phone or billing address, a vendor's name, email or phone, or an item's number, display name, description, purchase price, vendor or labor hours, doesn't come over FleetSuite's: it's listed to keep FleetSuite's, sent on to NetSuite (but for an item's vendor and labor hours, which stay in FleetSuite), or take NetSuite's ("FleetSuite's record wins") | The NetSuite customer, vendor and parts syncs, and Books → Changed in NetSuite |
| `customer_payments` | Anyone with Invoicing receives a customer's check or ACH payment and puts it on that customer's open invoices, and FleetSuite makes the payment in NetSuite, in Undeposited Funds ("Payments received in FleetSuite") | Invoicing → Invoiced → Payment on an open invoice |
| `customer_deposits` | Anyone with Invoicing takes a customer's check or ACH deposit on a sales order, and FleetSuite makes the customer deposit in NetSuite on that order, in Undeposited Funds; NetSuite puts it on the order's invoice when the order's billed ("Deposits taken in FleetSuite") | Deposit on a sales order, or on an estimate once it has its sales order |
| `bank_deposits` | Anyone with Invoicing deposits the customer payments and deposits waiting in Undeposited Funds to one of the company's bank accounts, ticked from what NetSuite has waiting, and FleetSuite makes the bank deposit in NetSuite ("Bank deposits made in FleetSuite") | Invoicing → Invoiced → Bank deposit |
| `vendor_payments` | Anyone with Payments pays the company's open bills in a payment run, ticked from what NetSuite has open, from one of its bank accounts, vendor by vendor by check or ACH, and asks for approval; a super admin who didn't ask approves it, and FleetSuite makes each vendor's bill payment in NetSuite ("Vendors paid in FleetSuite"); its checks print on BMG's check stock from the run, each number set in NetSuite ("Checks printed on BMG's stock"), and its ACH payments are paid from a file made for First Bank from the run ("The ACH file for First Bank") | More → Money & Books → Pay Vendors |
| `adjustment_approvals` | An inventory adjustment made with Adjust waits for approval: a super admin who didn't ask approves it, and FleetSuite makes it in NetSuite as an Inventory Adjustment, or turns it down saying why ("Adjustments approved before they're posted") | Adjust on the Inventory page or a part's details, and Adjustments to approve on the Inventory page |

## Posting

```ts
import { createServiceClient } from '@/lib/supabase-service';
import { postEntry } from '@/lib/books/journal';

const posted = await postEntry(createServiceClient(), {
  company: 'BMG',
  entryDate: '2027-01-04',
  sourceType: 'invoice',
  sourceId: invoice.id,
  postingRule: 'invoice@1',
  idempotencyKey: `invoice:${invoice.id}:1`,
  postedBy: { id: profile.id, name: profile.full_name },
  lines: [
    { accountId: ar.id, debit: 1250, customerId: invoice.customer_id, jobRef: `so:${so.id}` },
    { accountId: sales.id, credit: 1250, division: 'upfit', location: 'ofallon' },
  ],
});
```

- `idempotencyKey` names the document, the rule and the generation. Posting
  the same key again returns `alreadyPosted: true` and writes nothing.
- `postingRule` carries a version (`invoice@1`), so a change to how a
  document posts can be traced.
- Amounts may be numbers or plain decimal strings. They go to the database
  as exact two-decimal strings.
- Errors are `BooksError`s. `kind` is `invalid` (caught before the
  database), `refused` (a database rule, such as a closed month) or
  `failed` (anything else).

## Closing a month

`/admin/books/months` (Books → Months, part 44) closes a company's months
in FleetSuite's books once each is over, and reopens one for a reason
(`src/lib/books/months.ts`, `GET` and `POST /api/admin/books/months`). It
lists the months from the one the books went live in through this one,
newest first: whether each is closed and by whom, how it compares with
NetSuite as Differences vs NetSuite compares it (`comparedMonths`), and
every close and reopen with its reason (`gl_period_events`, migration 359).

The rules (`closeRefusal` and `reopenRefusal`):

- Nothing closes before the books are live, nor any month before the one
  they went live in: nothing posts to those but the opening entry.
- A month closes once its last day is over in Central time, and months
  close in order, so the first one open is the one to close.
- Closing a month that doesn't match NetSuite, or where NetSuite has
  documents of kinds FleetSuite doesn't post yet, needs a reason. One that
  matches doesn't.
- Only the latest month closed reopens, and only for a reason.

The bookkeeper and super admins close a month; only a super admin reopens
one ("Who can open the books").

Both are audited (`close_month`, `reopen_month`), and the journal keeps
who, when and why.

```ts
await setPeriodStatus(client, { company: 'BMG', month: '2027-01', status: 'closed', actor });
await setPeriodStatus(client, { company: 'BMG', month: '2027-01', status: 'open', actor, reason: 'Missed a vendor bill' });
```

A month opens the first time anything posts to it. A closed one takes
nothing new: the database refuses any entry dated in it. So the posting
run leaves a closed month as it stands. A document NetSuite changes, voids
or deletes there, one it books into or out of it, and a second entry
standing there all wait, counted on the run's record (`waiting`) with a
note for each month, and the first run after the month is reopened posts
them. The daily check still compares a closed month with NetSuite, and
when one stops matching, its notification says NetSuite's change waits
until the month is reopened. Differences vs NetSuite says so on a closed
month too.

Closing a month here doesn't close it in NetSuite, nor does NetSuite's
close reach FleetSuite: through 2027 NetSuite's months close there as
before.

Before closing a month, Inventory value shows the stock at its end tied to
each inventory account, every cent between them named ("Inventory value at
a month's end"); Months links to it.

## NetSuite feed

From January 2027 the team works only in FleetSuite and FleetSuite sends
each document on to NetSuite the moment someone saves it, so a send that
goes wrong needs to be seen, not just by whoever pressed. The NetSuite feed
(part 45, Books → NetSuite feed, `/admin/books/netsuite-feed`) keeps every
request FleetSuite makes that writes to NetSuite, with what NetSuite
answered (`netsuite_sends`, migration 477).

Every request to NetSuite goes through `netsuiteFetch`
(`src/lib/netsuite-sends.ts`) instead of `fetch`: each one in
`src/lib/netsuite.ts`, and the routes that sign their own (estimates push,
the invoice PO fix, the customer sync). A new write there is kept without
anything more to do. Reads go straight through: any GET, SuiteQL (a POST
that only reads) and the item script's ping. A write gets a row as it
starts and the whole row once NetSuite answers, and the caller still gets
exactly the answer or error `fetch` gave. Keeping it waits at most three
seconds once NetSuite has answered, and one that fails is a warning in the
log, never the send's problem.

Each send is one of:

- **Took it:** NetSuite answered 2xx. For a new record, NetSuite's ID for
  it comes from the answer's `Location`. The item script answers 200
  either way, so its answer's `success` says which.
- **Refused:** NetSuite answered 4xx, or the item script said it didn't do
  it. Nothing was made, so there's nothing to look for. NetSuite's reason
  is kept.
- **Not sure:** NetSuite answered 5xx or something FleetSuite couldn't
  read (as an item script answer that doesn't say `success`), or the
  request failed or timed out before an answer came. NetSuite may have done
  it anyway.
- **No answer:** still without an answer more than five minutes after it
  started (`NO_ANSWER_MS`), so the server stopped before NetSuite answered.
  It counts as not sure.

What's kept is never the request's body: the method, what it asked for (a
new record, a change, a delete, one made from another, as an invoice from
a sales order), NetSuite's name for the record, the record addressed or
made, one document number, external ID or name sent or answered to find it
by (`externalId`, `tranId`, `entityId`, `itemId` or `companyName`), the
HTTP status, NetSuite's reason with any run of nine or more digits hidden
(`scrub`), and who was signed in, read from the request's sign-in token (a
scheduled job's names nobody). That reads `next/headers`, loaded only as a
send starts: a few phone pages load the NetSuite client, and Next won't
build a page whose code imports `next/headers` at the top of a file
(`src/lib/client-graph.test.ts` finds one).

The page shows the last seven days' counts, the sends FleetSuite isn't sure
about that nobody has looked for yet, and every send, newest first, fifty at
a time, narrowed to one outcome or kind of record. Each links to the record
in NetSuite when FleetSuite knows its ID. One FleetSuite isn't sure about
stays at the top until the bookkeeper or a super admin taps Looked at it,
with a note of what they found in NetSuite
(`POST /api/admin/books/netsuite-feed`, audited `look_at_send`). Only one
NetSuite didn't settle can be marked, and only once. The daily check tells
the super admins about each of those once ("Daily check").

The feed records; it doesn't retry. A send that isn't sure is looked for in
NetSuite before anyone tries again, because sending it again is how a
second copy gets made.

## Going live

`/admin/books/going-live` (Books → Going live, parts 42 and 43) works out
the entry that would open a company's books the day after any month since
January 2025 ends, says what would stop it posting, and rehearses going
live after it. From December 1, 2026 it has the tap that sets the books
live, and once they are, the opening entries the posting run has posted.

FleetSuite's books already hold NetSuite's balances before 2025 (the
starting entry) and its own journal since, so the entry is only what still
differs from NetSuite's own totals on that month's last day, laid out as
the trial balance is (`workOutOpeningEntry` in
`src/lib/books/opening-balances.ts`): a line for each account that
differs, bringing it to NetSuite's balance, the year's income and expenses
on their own accounts and earlier years' net income on retained earnings.
So the books open at NetSuite's numbers line for line, and Reports, the
balances, the agings and the daily check carry on as they are, with the
journal's 2025 and 2026 history kept. The entry is dated that last day as
an opening balance, the one kind of entry the books take from before the
day they went live ("Rules the database enforces").

On the receivable and payable accounts it goes customer by customer and
vendor by vendor (part 43): migration 476's `gl_opening_parties` gives each
customer's and vendor's balance on those accounts through the day, from
the copy of NetSuite's ledger and from FleetSuite's journal, who a line is
for decided as Customer and vendor balances decides it (migration 406),
and the entry has a line for each one whose balance differs, carrying the
customer or vendor. So each one's balance opens at NetSuite's too. Such an
account can need lines when its own balance doesn't differ: one customer
too high by what another is too low.

What would stop it posting is listed with it: the month isn't over;
nothing has been copied from NetSuite's ledger; on the day the copy
differs from NetSuite's own totals (the entry is split by customer and
vendor from the copy's lines); NetSuite's balances don't balance; earlier
years' net income differs with no one retained earnings account to take
it; a line on an account the chart doesn't have yet, a heading, or an
account that's only for the other company; and a receivable or payable
account whose customers' or vendors' lines don't come to what it differs
by. The database refuses headings and other-company accounts anyway.

The rehearsal (`rehearse`): had the books gone live after that month, the
balances at the end of the latest month in FleetSuite's books, NetSuite's
numbers up to the day and FleetSuite's own journal since
(`gl_books_totals`, as Reports reads it), next to NetSuite's own, laid out
as the trial balance is. The entry makes the two agree on the day, so a
line differs only where FleetSuite has posted something differently since.

The page opens on the month before the live date (`books_live_from`, or
the planned `PLANNED_LIVE_FROM`, 2027-01-01) once it's over, and until then
on the same month a year earlier, a rehearsal of the same year end.

### The tap

From `GO_LIVE_FROM` (December 1, 2026) the page offers a super admin going
live, company by company: typing the company's code and Go live sets
`gl_companies.books_live_from` to January 1, 2027
(`POST /api/admin/books/going-live`, `goLiveRefusal` in
`src/lib/books/going-live.ts`), once, audited as `go_live`. It can't be
undone: migration 359 keeps the date, refuses anything dated before it but
opening balances, and refuses emptying the journal. So what NetSuite books
into December after the tap comes in with the opening entry rather than
document by document, and the nearer to December 31 the tap, the more of
December stays document by document.

### Once the books are live

- The posting run (`postInvoices`) leaves the months before the live date
  alone, and every entry dated before it: one whose document NetSuite
  voids or deletes stands, as does a second one standing for the same
  document (`standingDates`), and one whose document NetSuite moves into a
  month since posts afresh there instead of being replaced
  (`liveDecision`). The opening entry trues them up.
- The posting run posts the opening entry (`postOpeningEntries`), holding
  the books' lock, once the month before is over, nothing would stop it,
  and a whole copy of NetSuite's ledger has been made since that day ended
  in Central time (`openingWait`). Its key is
  `opening_balance:<company>:<live date>:<n>`. Whenever NetSuite's numbers
  for that day change afterwards (the CPA's year-end adjustments), what
  then differs posts as the next one, "Opening balances trued up". What
  waits or fails is a note on the run's record, with no amount in it, as
  the rest of that record has none (`failureReason` in
  `src/lib/books/journal.ts`): what would stop the entry is only counted
  there, and the Going live page says what it is.
  `gl_opening_entries` (migration 476) lists the ones posted, each with
  the entry that reversed it, if one did.
- The daily check looks only at the months since the live date. The
  months before stay as they were and the opening entry stands in the
  last of them, so Differences vs NetSuite shows that month differing by
  it ("Opening balance (FleetSuite only)").
- A document NetSuite moves from a month since back to before the live
  date keeps its entry in the month since: the daily check tells about
  it, and a journal entry in FleetSuite puts it right.

### The steps

1. Rehearse as often as needed, on the Going live page.
2. Late in December, go live on the Going live page, BMG and then 1084.
   This can't be undone.
3. Once December 31 is over and NetSuite's ledger has been copied whole
   since, the posting run posts each company's opening entry, and trues it
   up whenever NetSuite's December numbers change. The Going live page
   shows each one posted.

## 1099s

Books → 1099s (`/admin/books/1099s`, part 50) works out what each vendor
NetSuite marks to get a 1099 was paid in one company's year, from
FleetSuite's copy of NetSuite, the way NetSuite counts it. It's the NetSuite
Exit Checklist's 1099 dry run: in January 2027, 2026's totals are checked
against the 1099s NetSuite makes, before FleetSuite makes them itself later
in 2027. It only reads; nothing is filed or sent.

What it reads (`gl_1099_year`, migration 481):

- Which vendors get a 1099: the vendors copy's `is_1099`, `T` or `F`, with
  the legal name when it isn't the vendor's name ("NetSuite's lists").
- Which accounts count toward one: `gl_netsuite_1099_accounts`, each account
  NetSuite gives a 1099-MISC category, read with the chart ("Chart of
  accounts"). The category is the box of the form: Nonemployee
  Compensation, Rents and so on.
- The year's checks and bill payments, by the date paid, from the copies of
  them ("Bill payments", "Checks and card charges"), with their lines from
  the copy of NetSuite's ledger and, for a bill payment, the bills and bill
  credits it paid with theirs. Card charges and refunds come too, to be
  listed. One NetSuite voided or deleted is left out. Only what's for a
  vendor marked to get a 1099, or touches an account that counts, is read.

The rule (`buildYear1099`, `src/lib/books/1099s.ts`):

- A check counts on the accounts of its own lines, less the one it was paid
  from.
- A bill payment counts on the accounts of the bills it paid. What it paid
  on each bill, a bill credit it applied counting against them, is spread
  over that bill's lines by account, less payables, and the whole is brought
  to what the payment paid, in proportion to the cent, the leftover cents
  going to the largest remainders (`spread`), so the shares add up to the
  payment exactly. A discount taken for paying early isn't money paid, so
  it's in no box.
- Each account's share goes in its category's box. What's on other
  accounts goes in none.
- What's paid by card is left out, as the card company reports it on a
  1099-K: card charges and refunds, and checks and bill payments paid from a
  credit card account. It's listed instead.
- No minimum is applied: every vendor paid on an account that counts is
  shown, whatever the amount.

A payment that can't be split is listed with why (`unsplitReason`): it isn't
in the copy of the ledger yet, what it paid hasn't been copied, it paid a
bill from before 2025 (the ledger copy starts with 2025), it paid something
that isn't a bill (a journal entry, say), or NetSuite didn't say how much it
paid on one. One not in the copy of the ledger yet shows for both
companies, as which one it's for comes from its lines.

The page opens on last year for BMG, so through 2026 it shows 2025, which
can be checked against the 1099s filed in January 2026. It goes back to 2025
and up to this year in Central time, and has each company
(`GET /api/admin/books/1099s?company=&year=`, whoever opens the books). It
shows:

- The 1099s: each vendor marked to get one that was paid on an account that
  counts, a column for each category and a total, largest first. Tap one for
  its payments, each with what it put in which box.
- Paid on accounts that count, not marked to get a 1099 (red). One that
  should get a 1099 needs it ticked on its vendor record; one that
  shouldn't, an employee say, was likely paid on the wrong account. A payee
  that isn't in NetSuite's vendors goes by the payment's payee name.
- Payments FleetSuite can't split (red).
- Vendors marked to get a 1099 that were paid only on other accounts, and
  ones paid by card.
- The accounts that count, with their categories.
- Above them, why something may be missing: NetSuite wouldn't give the
  vendors, their 1099 flags or which accounts count; no account has a
  category; a copy failed; or checks and bill payments in the ledger copy
  for the year aren't in the copies of the payments yet (`not_copied`).

### NetSuite's 1099 report (part 51)

NetSuite's own 1099 report for the year can be dropped on the page, or
picked with "tap to choose the file", to see it next to FleetSuite's totals.
In NetSuite, open the 1099 report for the year and use Export – CSV; an
Excel `.xlsx` works too. NetSuite's Excel export is an old `.xls` FleetSuite
can't read, and says so.

The file is read on the phone or computer it's picked on
(`read1099ReportFile`, `src/lib/books/1099-report.ts`) and never sent:

- The headings are the first row, within the first 30, naming the vendor
  (Vendor, Name, Payee...) and at least one box or a total. Rows above them
  are the report's title, kept to show which report it was. A box column is
  matched to a 1099-MISC category by name without case, punctuation, "Box
  7" or "1099-NEC" (`boxKey`): FleetSuite's own category names first, then
  the form's. A Legal Name, Print on Check As or Vendor ID column is kept as
  another name for matching.
- Everything else is left out and only its heading is kept, to show what was
  left out: a tax ID whatever its heading says (Tax ID, TIN, SSN, EIN,
  Federal ID...), an address, a phone. A name column with nine digits in a
  row in any cell is left out whole, and so is a row naming its vendor only
  by nine digits. Any run of nine digits, one character apart at most
  (`123-45-6789`, `12 3456789`), is taken out of whatever is kept (`scrub`),
  so **a vendor's tax ID never reaches FleetSuite**. The route refuses one
  too (`checkReport`), and so do the save function and the table
  (`gl_1099_report_has_nine_digits`). Amounts are numbers, not text, so a
  million dollars (nine digits of cents) is fine.
- Amounts read as `$1,234.56`, `(1,234.56)` or `-1234.56`. A cell under a
  box that isn't an amount stops it, saying which row, and nothing is kept.
- A total row ("Total", "Grand Total", "Total - Sample Painting") is
  skipped; a vendor called "Total Signs" isn't. A vendor listed again is
  added to its first row. A row of amounts with no vendor is left out and
  counted, as a detail report's rows are, and the page says to use the
  summary report.

What's kept (`gl_netsuite_1099_reports`, migration 482, one a company's
year, a new upload replacing it): the file's name, the title lines, the
columns kept with the box each is for, the headings left out, each vendor's
names and amounts in cents by column, what was skipped, and who uploaded it
when (`POST /api/admin/books/1099s` with `{ company, year, report }`;
`{ company, year, remove: true }` takes it off; both audited without
amounts, the bookkeeper's and super admins' only).

The comparison (`compare1099`) is worked out each time the page opens, so
it follows the copies as they come up to date:

- A report vendor is FleetSuite's when its name, or failing that one of its
  other names, is one of a FleetSuite vendor's names: its name, legal name,
  NetSuite id, or id and name together (`V-50 Sample Painting`), compared
  without case or punctuation. Failing those, the first word of its name is
  tried when it has a digit in it, as NetSuite puts a vendor's id before its
  name (`V1001 Sample Person` for a vendor FleetSuite knows only as `V1001`,
  a person with no company name, say). One that fits more than one is listed
  with the names it could be, unless another of its names says which. Report
  rows that are all one vendor are added up.
- Each box column is compared with FleetSuite's category of the same name,
  and the total with everything FleetSuite counted; with no total column,
  NetSuite's total is its boxes added up, so a box the report lacks shows as
  a difference.
- The page shows a line of how many match to the cent, differ, are only in
  NetSuite's report, and are FleetSuite's 1099s not in it; then the vendors
  that differ (red), each opening to its boxes side by side, and noting
  when FleetSuite couldn't split some of what it was paid or the vendor
  isn't marked to get a 1099; the vendors only in NetSuite's report; the
  ones only in FleetSuite's (NetSuite's report may leave out a vendor paid
  under $600; FleetSuite applies no minimum); the matches; and what was
  read and left out. A title naming another year is pointed out.

### What isn't done yet

- Making the 1099s and e-filing them (the checklist's July to September
  2027), with this rule. FleetSuite keeps no vendor's tax ID.

## Who can open the books

Part 52 (migration 483, `src/lib/books/access.ts`) opens the books to two
logins besides super admins, each its own role that only a super admin
gives, so an outside bookkeeper or CPA has a login of their own rather than
a staff one:

| Who | Level | What they can do |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Super admin | `owner` | Everything, including the steps only the owner takes: the switches, going live, keeping FleetSuite's or taking NetSuite's on Changed in NetSuite, and reopening a closed month |
| Bookkeeper | `keep` | Everything else: Sync now and Copy now, Post now, Look now, closing a month, clearing and reopening mistake checks, Looked at it on the NetSuite feed, Snap a bill, and NetSuite's 1099 report |
| CPA | `read` | Every Books page and Excel download, changing nothing, and its own adjustments journal on Journal entries ("The CPA's adjustments journal") |

How it's held:

- Every Books route checks the caller's own roles with
  `requireBooks(req, need)` (`src/lib/api-auth.ts`), each step needing
  `read`, `keep` or `owner` as the route manifest says
  (`src/lib/route-permissions.ts`, kind `books`). A step the caller can't
  take answers 403 with why.
- Each page opens to whoever `booksAccess` lets in and shows a button only
  to whoever can use it, by the roles it's viewed as: a super admin's View
  As → Bookkeeper or CPA shows that login's pages, while the server still
  answers by the super admin's own roles. View As offers the two to super
  admins only.
- A link from a Books page to one outside the books (Vendor Details, Item
  Accounts, Fix a bill, Receiving, an AP invoice, a payout, Parts Mail)
  shows only to whoever can open that page.
- Neither role is BMG staff (`INTERNAL_STAFF_ROLES`, `is_internal_staff()`),
  and neither carries a feature but Home, so nothing outside the books opens
  to them; Home opens the books for a login with no other role. Stack
  another role on a person who needs more.
- Only a super admin gives the super admin, bookkeeper or CPA role: the
  Users page greys the chip for anyone else, Add user refuses it
  (`/api/admin/create-user`), and the database refuses it
  (`guard_profile_privilege_columns`, migration 483), while an admin can
  still take one away. Sign-up can't ask for either.
- The AI assistant isn't open to a login that has nothing but these roles.

### What isn't done yet

- The books' alerts (the daily check's, and with it the mistake checks' and
  the NetSuite feed's) still go to super admins only.
